), we notice that by impossibility of frequency interactions, we must have

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)]\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}},

and thus by (
3.25
), (
3.26
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg(\sum_{N}N^{2s}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}&\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\bigg(\sum_{N}N^{2s}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F})\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\big[\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}}\big].\end{split}

(3.28)

Next, we consider
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}_{N}
in (
3.21
). Using Bernstein’s inequality and (
2.10
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{M}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{M}[\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}e^{i\beta F}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{M}u]\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{M}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}e^{i\beta F}\cdot u]\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{M}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+M^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}e^{i\beta F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+M^{-\frac{1}{2}+}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}.

(3.29)

Therefore, if
s<1
, we have from (
2.10
) and (
3.29
) that

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}_{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N_{1}\gtrsim N\vee N_{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}e^{-i\beta F}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-1+}T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}),

while if
1\leq s<2
, we instead use (
2.11
) which implies

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}_{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}N^{-s-}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s-\frac{3}{4}+}_{x}}).

Combining the two estimates above, we get

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{N}N^{2s}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}_{N}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{5})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}}.

(3.30)

Therefore, the first estimate in (
3.15
) follows once we combine (
3.21
), (
3.23
),
(
3.28
),
(
3.27
),
(
3.30
),
together
with
(
3.20
) for the
v
term in (
3.23
) and (
3.2
) for the last term in (
3.21
).
The second estimate in (
3.15
) follows from the approach above, since the recovery formula (
3.13
) extends to
\widetilde{u}
by replacing all
v,w
with
\widetilde{v},\widetilde{w}
, where we apply (
3.2
) to the corresponding
\widetilde{v}
term in (
3.23
) and to the
\widetilde{w}
term in (
3.21
) as follows

\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\widetilde{w}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}}_{T,x}}=\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}w\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}_{T}},

completing the proof of (
3.15
).

We now move on to the first estimate in (
3.16
). We first control the high-frequency portion of
u
using (
3.13
). If we followed the same strategy as in (
3.21
), we would no longer obtain extra factors of
T
from the terms
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}-\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt V}
, so we need to refine the estimates for those terms. Moreover, our estimate for
I
includes contributions from the norms of
u
which causes issues in the bootstrap argument if we do not assume small data. We need to ensure there is a small factor at the front of such terms. Fix
M\in\mathbb{N}
large, and decompose
u=\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}u+\mathbf{P}_{>M}u
.
For the contribution from
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}u
, we use the Duhamel formula for
u
and Hölder’s inequality in time to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim&\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+T^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}[u\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})]\|_{L^{4}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\\
&+T\big\{\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}(u\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2}))\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}(|u|^{2}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\big\}.\end{split}

(3.31)

The third and fourth terms in (
3.31
) are easily bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}TM^{s+1}(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3},\end{split}

which can be proved easily using similar ideas to Lemma
2.2
(i).
Then, using the projection
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}
followed by (
2.5
), Sobolev embedding, and Minkowski’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}[u\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})]\|_{L^{4}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}\partial_{x}[u\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u|^{2})]\|_{L^{4}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}[\partial_{x}u\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u|^{2})]\|_{L^{4}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{1+s}\big\{\|u\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{4}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{x}u\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{4}_{T}H^{-1}_{x}}\big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{3}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T}\widetilde{L^{4}_{x}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{3}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}),

using
s\leq 2
.
Combining these estimates, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}).

(3.32)

Now we consider the contribution from
\mathbf{P}_{>M}u
for which we use (
3.13
), leading to the terms in (
3.21
) with this additional projection
\mathbf{P}_{>M}
and in
L^{\infty}_{T}H_{x}^{s}
.
For the contribution of the first term in (
3.13
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}J_{x}^{s}&\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F}v]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}J_{x}^{s}[\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}v]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}J_{x}^{s}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F})v]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.33)

For the first term in (
3.33
), by the fractional Leibniz rule, and (
2.11
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}J_{x}^{s}[\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}v]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}(e^{-i\beta F})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{4}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{4}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}(e^{-i\beta F})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{4}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}})\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},\end{split}

(3.34)

for
s<2
.
Next, in a similar way, for the second term in (
3.33
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\mathbf{P}_{>M}J_{x}^{s}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F})v]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}e^{-i\beta F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2+}_{x}}\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty-}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}e^{-i\beta F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|J^{s}_{x}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim M^{-\theta}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}}\|{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},\end{split}

(3.35)

for some
\theta>0
,
where we used (
2.11
).

For the contribution from the second term in (
3.13
), from the
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}
and for
M
sufficiently large, we note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{>M}

\displaystyle J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}.

Then, we use the fractional Leibniz rule and similar estimates as in (
3.35
) to bound this by

\displaystyle\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(

\displaystyle e^{-i\beta F})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-\theta}(1+\|u\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}},

(3.36)

for some
\theta>0
.
In the course of this estimate, we have also essentially bounded the contribution coming from the similar third term in (
3.13
).
For the fourth term in (
3.13
), we note that frequency signs imply

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{+}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F}u)]=\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{+}\mathbf{P}_{>M}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F}u)],

for which we proceed as the first term in (
3.35
) with Sobolev inequality to get

\displaystyle\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{+}(e^{-i\beta F})\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F}u)]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{+}\mathbf{P}_{>M}e^{-i\beta F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2+}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F}u)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty-}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim M^{-\theta}(1+\|u\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\max(\frac{1}{2},s-\frac{3}{4}+)}_{x}}.

(3.37)

Then, the first estimate in (
3.16
) follows from combining
(
3.32
), (
3.13
), (
3.33
)-(
3.35
), (
3.36
), (
3.37
), together with applying (
3.1
) to the last term in (
3.13
) and to the
v
terms in in (
3.34
)-(
3.35
), after replacing
\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}}
with
\|\widetilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}
.

For the second estimate in (
3.16
), we handle the low frequency part
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}\widetilde{u}
by using the Duhamel formulation for (
3.9
), where we estimate the last contribution in (
3.9
) by

\displaystyle T\|M(\widetilde{u})\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}\partial_{x}\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim TM^{2}\|\widetilde{u}\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

The high frequency part is handled as for
\mathbf{P}_{>M}u
, only changing the estimate for the last term in (
3.13
), where we first replace the
\widetilde{w}
-norm by
w
and then use (
3.1
).
This completes the proof of (
3.16
). ∎

We now extend (
3.14
), (
3.15
), and (
3.16
)
to difference estimates.

Lemma 3.6

(Difference estimates)
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<2
,
0\leq T\leq 1
, and
u_{j}
be smooth solutions to (
1.4
) on
[0,T]
with initial data
u_{j}(0)=u_{0,j}
,
j=1,2
. Let
F_{j}=F_{j}[u_{j}]
be as defined in (
3.4
), set
v_{j}=v_{j}[u_{j}]
and
w_{j}=w_{j}[u_{j}]
as in (
3.5
),
U:=u_{1}-u_{2}
and
W:=w_{1}-w_{2}
.
Also, given
\widetilde{u}_{j}:=\mathcal{J}_{u_{j}}[u_{j}]
and
\widetilde{v}_{j}:=\mathcal{J}_{u_{j}}[v_{j}]
as in (
3.8
),
j=1,2
, set

\widetilde{V}:=\widetilde{v}_{1}-\widetilde{v}_{2}:=\mathcal{J}_{u_{1}}[v_{1}]-\mathcal{J}_{u_{2}}[v_{2}]
.
Lastly, let
R_{s_{0}}:=R_{s_{0}}(u_{1},u_{2}):=\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+\|u_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}
and
\sigma:=\max(\tfrac{1}{2},s-\tfrac{3}{4}+).

(i)
The following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq 1}\|U\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta,\theta}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{0}(u_{1},u_{2},U)\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq 1}\|\widetilde{U}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta,\theta}_{T}}\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{0}(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2},\widetilde{U}),

(3.38)

where
G_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{0}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2},\mathbf{U})

\displaystyle:=\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}})R_{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big\{R_{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\mathbf{U}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\max_{j=1,2}\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\mathbf{u}_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\Big\}.

(ii)

Given
\theta>0
,
M,\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, we define the quantities

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{1}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle:=T^{\frac{1}{8}}\big[1+Q\big(R_{\sigma}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})\big)\big]\Big\{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|\mathbf{W}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{z}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\big[\|e^{i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{1}]}-e^{i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{2}]}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\sigma}_{x}}\big](1+\|J^{s}\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}})\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{\frac{1}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}\big|M\big(\mathbf{u}_{1}(0)\big)-M\big(\mathbf{u}_{2}(0)\big)\big|\|\mathbf{z}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{2}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle:=\|\mathbf{U}(0)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{V}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|\mathbf{W}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}})\big(1+R^{2}_{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})+\max_{j=1,2}\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\mathbf{u}_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big\{\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{U}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|e^{\pm i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{1}]}-e^{\pm i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{2}]}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+M^{-\theta}Q\big(R_{\sigma}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})\big)\Big\{\|\mathbf{V}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|e^{i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{1}]}-e^{i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{2}]}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{1}]}-e^{-i\beta F[\mathbf{u}_{2}]}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda T\big|M(\mathbf{u}_{1}(0))-M(\mathbf{u}_{2}(0))\big|\|\mathbf{z}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{z}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}},

where
Q
is a polynomial of degree at least 1,
F
is as in (
3.4
), and
M(\mathbf{u})
denotes the mass of
\mathbf{u}
as in (
1.13
).
Then, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}U\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2},\widetilde{v}_{1},\widetilde{v}_{1},U,\widetilde{V},W),

(3.39)

\displaystyle\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\widetilde{U}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{1}(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2},\widetilde{v}_{1},w_{1},\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W),

(3.40)

\displaystyle\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{2}(u_{1},u_{2},\widetilde{v}_{1},\widetilde{v}_{1},U,\widetilde{V},W),

(3.41)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{G}_{2}(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2},\widetilde{v}_{1},w_{1},\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W).

(3.42)

Proof.

We follow the same strategy as for Lemma
3.5
.
To estimate
\|U\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta,\theta}_{T}}
in (
3.38
), we proceed as in the proof of (
3.14
),
which reduces to controlling

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}U+i\partial_{x}^{2}U\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{1}\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u_{1}|^{2})-u_{2}\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{+}(|u_{2}|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|u_{1}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{1}|^{2})-u_{2}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{2}|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\||u_{1}|^{2}u_{1}-|u_{2}|^{2}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}},

and each of these can be estimated as in the proof of (
3.14
).
In the estimate for
\|\widetilde{U}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta,\theta}_{T}}
, we proceed as above, and use the equation (
3.9
) for
\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2}
, which contributes with the additional term

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M(\widetilde{u}_{1})\partial_{x}\widetilde{u}_{1}-\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M(\widetilde{u}_{2})\partial_{x}\widetilde{u}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq|M(\widetilde{u}_{1})-M(\widetilde{u}_{2})|\|\widetilde{u}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+|M(\widetilde{u}_{2})|\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}R_{\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}},

completing the proof of (
3.38
) for
\widetilde{U}
.

We move onto (
3.39
). Letting
F_{j}:=F[u_{j}]
,
j=1,2
, we see from (
3.13
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}U=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})v_{1}]+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F_{2}}V]

(3.43)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F_{1}}u_{1})\big]

(3.44)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}((e^{i\beta F_{1}}-e^{i\beta F_{2}})u_{1})\big]

(3.45)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}(e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{+,\text{lo}}(e^{i\beta F_{2}}U)\big]

(3.46)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}}]\mathbf{P}_{0}(e^{i\beta F_{1}}u_{1})+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F_{2}}]\mathbf{P}_{0}((e^{i\beta F_{1}}-e^{i\beta F_{2}})u_{1})

(3.47)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F_{2}}]\mathbf{P}_{0}(e^{i\beta F_{2}}U)

(3.48)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{+}(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F_{1}}u_{1})\big]

(3.49)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{+}(e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{-}((e^{i\beta F_{1}}-e^{i\beta F_{2}})u_{1})\big]

(3.50)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}\big[\mathbf{P}_{+}(e^{-i\beta F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{-}(e^{i\beta F_{2}}U)\big]

(3.51)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}W.

(3.52)

We then estimate each of these terms similarly to how we did for (
3.15
) and with the additional terms in (first one in) (
3.43
), (
3.44
), (
3.45
), (
3.47
), (
3.49
), and (
3.50
), for which we use the difference estimates (
2.12
) and (
2.13
).
We omit the similar details and note the only change in handling the second term in (
3.43
).

Proceeding as in the proof of (
3.15
), namely the estimate for
\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt_{N}
in (
3.23
), we have

\displaystyle\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[e^{-i\beta F_{2}}V]\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{8}}\big(1+R_{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{1},u_{2})\big)\big(1+R_{\sigma}(u_{1},u_{2})\big)\|J^{s}_{x}V\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}.

Note that we would like to replace
V
by
\widetilde{V}
above, but (
3.20
) does not apply directly to the differences
V
and
\widetilde{V}
, since
u_{1},u_{2}
may have distinct mass
M_{j}:=M(u_{j})
,
j=1,2
; see (
3.8
). Instead, we expand
V
further and consider a high and low-frequency decomposition. First, we write

\displaystyle V(t,x)

\displaystyle=(v_{1}-v_{2})(t,x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}_{u_{1}}[v_{1}](t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-\mathcal{J}_{u_{2}}[v_{2}](t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}_{u_{1}}[v_{1}](t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-\mathcal{J}_{u_{1}}[v_{1}](t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)+(\mathcal{J}_{u_{1}}[v_{1}]-\mathcal{J}_{u_{2}}[v_{2}])(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)+(\widetilde{v}_{1}-\widetilde{v}_{2})(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t),

(3.53)

and then consider a high and low-frequency decomposition of the first contribution to obtain

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}V\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|J^{s}_{x}\widetilde{V}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|\Pi_{\leq\Lambda}[\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)]\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}[\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-\widetilde{v}_{1}(t,x+\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)]\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|J^{s}_{x}\widetilde{V}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{\frac{1}{4}}\|(e^{-it\frac{\beta}{\pi}(M_{1}-M_{2})\xi}-1)|\mathbf{1}_{|\xi|\leq\Lambda}\langle\xi\rangle^{s}{\mathcal{F}}_{x}(\widetilde{v}_{1})(t,\xi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\ell^{2}_{\xi}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|J^{s}_{x}\widetilde{V}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}\|M_{1}-M_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}}\|\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+T^{\frac{1}{8}}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{8}}\big\{\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}+\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}\big|M\big(u_{1}(0)\big)-M\big(u_{2}(0)\big)\big|\times\|\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}\big\},

where
\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Pi_{\leq\Lambda}
and
\Pi_{>\Lambda}
are the sharp frequency projectors, and we used (
3.1
), mass conservation on the second contribution, and (
3.1
) on the third.
The estimate in (
3.39
) for
U
follows.

Similarly,
for the
\widetilde{U}
estimate in (
3.40
), we use the recovery formula in (
3.13
) for
\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2}
and obtain an expansion for
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\widetilde{U}
, where most terms can be handled as in the proof of (
3.39
). Here, the second term in (
3.43
) is replaced by
\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{HI}}[e^{-i\beta F[\widetilde{u}_{2}]}\widetilde{V}]
which can be estimated as
\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt_{N}
in (
3.23
). Instead, we need an analogous decomposition to (
3.53
) for the last term
\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\widetilde{W}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{u_{1}}[w_{1}]-\mathcal{T}_{u_{2}}[w_{2}]=w_{1}(t,x-\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{1}t)-w_{1}(t,x-\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t)+W(t,x-\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}M_{2}t),

which we can estimate as above to obtain

\displaystyle\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}\widetilde{W}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{8}}\big\{\|W\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}\big|M\big(u_{1}(0)\big)-M\big(u_{2}(0)\big)\big|\|w_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}}\big\}.

Now we consider (
3.41
). We split
U=\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}U+\mathbf{P}_{>M}U
.
For the contribution
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}U
we use the Duhamel formula for
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}U
and gain a factor of
T
at the cost of a positive power of

M
, as in (
3.32
)
We omit the similar details.

For the contribution due to
\mathbf{P}_{>M}U
, we adapt the argument used for establishing (
3.39
).
For the terms (
3.44
)-(
3.51
), we proceed as in the proof of (
3.39
) with (
2.12
)-(
2.13
) instead of (
2.10
)-(
2.11
) whenever we see a difference of exponentials,
which always leads to a gain of
M^{-\theta}
.
For (
3.52
), there is no gain of powers of
M
. For the second term in (
3.43
), we proceed as in (
3.33
)-(
3.35
), together with (
3.53
) to see a
\widetilde{V}
contribution.

It only remains to more carefully estimate the first term in (
3.43
).
We write it as in (
3.33
):

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})v_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})v_{1}]

(3.54)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})v_{1}].

For the second term, as in (
3.35
), we gain a negative power of
M
due to the inner projection:

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{>M}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,HI}}[\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}(e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}})v_{1}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-\theta}Q\big(R_{\sigma}(u_{1},u_{2})\big)\big\{\|U\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\sigma}_{x}}+\|e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\big\}\|\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}},

for some polynomial
Q
of degree at least one, where we used (
2.12
)-(
2.13
) and (
3.1
).
For the first term in (
3.54
) we keep both of the projections.
This completes the proof of (
3.41
).

The proof of (
3.42
) follows similarly to that of (
3.41
). For the low frequency part
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}\widetilde{U}
, we use the Duhamel formula for
\widetilde{U}
corresponding to (
3.9
). The only distinction is an additional contribution coming from the last term in (
3.9
), which we control as follows

\displaystyle T\big\|\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}\big(M_{1}\partial_{x}\widetilde{u}_{1}-M_{2}\partial_{x}\widetilde{u}_{2}\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim TM^{3}Q\big(R_{\frac{1}{2}}(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{2})\big)\big\{\big|M(u_{1}(0))-M(u_{2}(0))\big|+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\big\}.

Similarly, for the large frequency part
\mathbf{P}_{>M}\widetilde{U}
, we use the decomposition in (
3.43
)-(
3.52
), with
\widetilde{U}
,
\widetilde{V}
,
\widetilde{W}
,
\widetilde{F}_{j}
,
j=1,2
, and
\widetilde{u}_{1}
, proceeding as for (
3.41
). However, we do not need to proceed via (
3.53
) since we already see
\widetilde{V}
term, and instead use the analogous rewriting to replace
\widetilde{W}
by
W
, completing the proof. ∎

4.
Trilinear estimates

We now show the crucial trilinear estimates.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{16}
, and
0<T\leq 1
. Then, for any
v,w\in X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}
, and
u_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cap X_{T}^{-\frac{1}{2},1}
with
J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}u_{j}\in\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}
,
j=2,3
,
it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}}B(u_{2},u_{3}),

(4.1)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}[w\,\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\|w\|_{X_{T}^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}}B(u_{2},u_{3}),

(4.2)

where

\displaystyle B(u_{2},u_{3}):=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=2}^{3}\big(\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|u_{j}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}_{T}}+\|u_{j}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{8},\frac{3}{8}}_{T}}\big)

\displaystyle+\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{2,3\}\\
j_{1}\neq j_{2}\end{subarray}}\|u_{j_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big\{\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u_{j_{2}}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|u_{j_{2}}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}_{T}}\Big\}.

(4.3)

Proof.

We first consider (
4.1
). We take any extension
u_{j}^{{\dagger}}
of
u_{j}
on
[0,T]
,
j=2,3
. To simplify notation, we simply write
u_{j}^{{\dagger}}
as
u_{j}
.
By duality and dyadic decomposition, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[v\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}}}\leq 1}\bigg|\sum_{N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}v)\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt\bigg|.

(4.4)

The projections
\mathbf{P}_{+}
and
\mathbf{P}_{-}
here imply that:

\displaystyle N_{1}\gtrsim N_{23}\vee N.

(4.5)

In particular,
N_{1}\gtrsim 1
.
Thus, we control (
4.4
) under the additional restriction (
4.5
). We let
N_{\max}:=\max(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23},N).

We also note that

\displaystyle N_{23}\lesssim N_{2}\vee N_{3}.

(4.6)

The function
\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}v
will always be placed in
X^{s,b}
for some
0<b<\frac{1}{2}
. As multiplication by the sharp cut-off
\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}
is a bounded operator in these spaces, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}v\|_{X^{s,b}}\lesssim\|v\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}.

We will apply this inequality without further mention in the following, and to simplify notation, we denote
\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}v
by
v_{T}
.

Case 1:

N_{2}\sim N_{3}
:

By Hölder’s inequality and (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt\bigg|\\
&\lesssim N^{s}N_{23}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim N^{s}N_{23}N_{1}^{-s}(N_{2}N_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

(4.7)

To sum over the dyadics, we first have for
(N_{2},N_{3},N_{23})

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{2}\sim N_{3}}

\displaystyle\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\sum_{N_{23}\lesssim N_{2}}\frac{N_{23}}{N_{2}}\lesssim\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{2}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{t,x}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{3}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{t,x}}}.

Meanwhile, for
(N,N_{1})
, with (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\sum_{N_{1}\gtrsim N}\bigg(\frac{N}{N_{1}}\bigg)^{s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{-sk}\sum_{N}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{2^{k}N}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}.

This is acceptable as long as
\delta_{0}\leq\frac{1}{16}
.

Case 2:

N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}\gtrsim N
:

In this case, we can also proceed by using (
4.4
) and the
L^{4}
-argument as in (
4.7
). By (
4.5
), we must have
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\vee N_{3}\sim N_{23}\sim N_{\max}
. Without loss of generality, assume that
N_{2}\vee N_{3}=N_{2}
. Then we bound this contribution to (
4.4
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N,N_{3}\\
N_{3}\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle N^{s}N_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\sum_{N_{1}\sim N_{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\sum_{N_{23}\gtrsim N}N_{23}N_{1}^{-s}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{2}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{t,x}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N,N_{3}\\
N_{3}\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{N}{N_{3}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{3}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|v_{T}\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{2}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{t,x}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{3}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{t,x}}}.

Case 3:

N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\wedge N_{3}\ll N
:

Here, we
need to make use of the phase function, since the weight
(N_{2}\land N_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in Case 2 is not useful.
We note the following resonance identity

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3}-\sigma=\xi_{1}^{2}-\xi_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2}-\xi^{2}=-2(\xi-\xi_{1})(\xi-\xi_{3}):=\Phi(\overline{\xi}),

where
\sigma=\tau-\xi^{2}
,
\sigma_{j}=\tau_{j}-\xi_{j}^{2}
,
j=1,2,3
,
\tau=\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}
, and
\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}
.
Note that under the last condition, we have
\xi-\xi_{1}=\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\max}:=\max(|\sigma_{1}|,|\sigma_{2}|,|\sigma_{3}|,|\sigma|)\gtrsim(N_{2}\vee N_{3})|\xi-\xi_{3}|\gtrsim(N_{2}\vee N_{3})(N\lor N_{3})=:K,

(4.8)

and we note that

\displaystyle N_{1}\sim N_{\max}.

(4.9)

In (
4.4
) we sum over
N_{23}
as we no longer need to use this dyadic restriction.
For fixed
(N,N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
and
K
as in (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt

(4.10)

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt

(4.11)

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt

(4.12)

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt

(4.13)

\displaystyle\hphantom{X}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{s}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3})]dxdt,

(4.14)

where the projections
\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}
are as in (
2.3
).
The terms in (
4.10
)–(
4.13
) are the non-resonant contributions while (
4.14
) is the nearly-resonant contribution.

Bounds for (
4.10
), (
4.11
), and (
4.14
):

The bounds for (
4.10
)-(
4.11
) follow exactly as in
[
10
, Bounds for (4.13)-(4.14) on pp.41-42]
, while (
4.14
) vanishes as in
[
10
, (4.21)]
. We omit details.

Bound for (
4.12
):

By Hölder’s inequality, (
3.2
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&N^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})\big\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g\big\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{N^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{N_{1}^{s}K}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.\end{split}

(4.15)

We now control the ensuing multiplier. If
N\gtrsim N_{3}
, then,

\displaystyle N^{s}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-s}K^{-1}\lesssim\begin{cases}N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad&\text{if}\quad s\geq 1,\\
N^{-1+s}N_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\quad&\text{if}\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1,\end{cases}

(4.16)

which always gives a negative power of
N_{\max}
, unless in the case
s=\frac{1}{2}
and
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\gg N\gg N_{3}
, where we instead use
N^{-\frac{1}{2}}
to sum over
(N,N_{3})
and then the Cauchy-Schwarz argument to sum over
(N_{1},N_{2})
under
N_{1}\sim N_{2}
.
The remaining case when
N\ll N_{3}
is better. It follows that

N_{2}\ll N\ll N_{3}\sim N_{1}
, and we argue as in (
4.15
) with

\displaystyle\text{LHS of }\eqref{X2mult}\lesssim N_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{3}^{-1}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}},

from which the estimate easily follows.

Bound for (
4.13
):

Recall that
N_{1}\sim N_{\max}
.

\bullet

Case A:

N_{3}\gg N_{2}
:

Fix
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{3}
. Note that by (
4.8
), we have
K\gtrsim N_{3}^{2}\gg N_{2}^{2}
.
By Hölder’s inequality, (
3.2
), Bernstein’s inequality, and Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle N^{s}N_{3}\|\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{2}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{3}\|_{L^{2(1+\varepsilon)}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{s}N_{3}N_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}N_{1}^{-s}K^{-1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}},

where the power of
N_{\max}
is always negative and we can easily perform all of the dyadic summations.

\bullet

Case B:

N_{3}\ll N_{2}
:

Fix
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small to be chosen later.
By duality, Hölder’s inequality, (
3.2
), Bernstein’s inequality, and Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle N^{s}N_{2}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}g\|_{L^{\frac{2(1+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}v_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\ll K}u_{2}\|_{L_{t,x}^{4}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}u_{3}\|_{L^{2(1+\varepsilon)}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{s+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}N_{2}N_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{8}}K^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}K^{2\delta_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}v_{T}\|_{X^{s,\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u_{3}\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2},1}},

by taking
0<\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}\leq 2\delta_{0}
.
We now consider the dyadic multiplier, for which we have

\displaystyle\frac{N^{s+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}N_{2}N_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{8}}K^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}K^{2\delta_{0}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}\lesssim\frac{N^{s-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}N_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2(1+\varepsilon)}}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{\frac{1}{8}-2\delta_{0}}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{8}+6\delta_{0}},

by considering
N_{1}\sim N
or
N_{1}\sim N_{2}
, for small
\varepsilon>0
as above and
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{12}
.
Again, this factor can then deal with the dyadic summations as for any
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
, we gain a negative power of
N_{\max}
.

The proof of (
4.2
) follows exactly the same arguments as (
4.1
) in view of the following observation:
on the support of
\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}[w\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(\overline{u_{2}}u_{3})]
, we must have

|\xi_{1}|=|\xi|+|\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}|,

which leads to (
4.5
) after dyadic decompositions in space.
∎

We now bound the remaining easier terms on the right-hand sides of (
3.6
) and (
3.7
).

Lemma 4.2
.

Given
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{4}
,
0<T\leq 1
, and
\mathcal{Q}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}[e^{i\beta F}u\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}[\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}+\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{\textup{op}}]\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3}(1+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\big(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|J^{s}_{x}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\big),

(4.17)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}[u\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}[\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}+\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{\textup{op}}]\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\|J^{s}_{x}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}.

(4.18)

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}f\|_{X^{s,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta_{0}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},

(4.19)

the estimates in (
4.17
)-(
4.18
) follow easily from first applying (
4.19
)
and then using the fractional Leibniz rule, Sobolev embedding, (
3.1
), (
1.3
), and (
2.18
).
From (
2.18
), we reduce to estimating

\|u\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s_{1}}_{x}}

for
s_{1}\in\{\frac{1}{2},s\}
. By the fractional Leibniz rule, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\|u\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s_{1}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|J^{s_{1}}_{x}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|u\|_{L^{6}_{T,x}}\|J^{s_{1}}_{x}\mathcal{Q}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{3}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\|J_{x}^{s_{1}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{3}\to L^{3}}\|u\|_{L^{6}_{T,x}}\|J_{x}^{s_{1}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|u\|_{L^{12}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}(\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{\textup{op}}+\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\|J_{x}^{s_{1}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}},

(4.20)

where the control of
\|\mathcal{Q}\|_{L^{3}\to L^{3}}
in terms of the
L^{2}\to L^{2}
and
L^{4}\to L^{4}
operator norms, comes from interpolation.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

It is clear that the results of Lemma
4.2
generalise to multilinear estimates.

5.
Well-posedness

By adapting the argument in
[
26
, Section 2.1]
with
[
50
, Lemma 5.4]
, we have the following local well-posedness result for (
1.1
) in high regularity.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
s>\frac{3}{2}
. Then, given
R>0
, there is
T=T(R)>0
such that for every
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
with
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\leq R
, there exists a unique solution
u\in C([-T,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
of (
1.1
) with
u(0)=u_{0}
. Moreover, the flow map
u_{0}\mapsto u(t)
is continuous from
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
to
C([-T,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
.

Our goal in this section is to extend the local well-posedness result in Proposition
5.1
to all regularities
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Namely,
an improvement by a full derivative below this previous result. Our estimates in the previous sections are sensitive to this gap and (mildly) change between the regimens
s\geq 1
and
s<1
;
see for example (
3.15
) and (
3.16
).
Thus, in order to give a rigorous proof of Theorem
1.1
, we need a two-stage argument:

(1)

Extend the result of Proposition
5.1
to
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for any
1\leq s\leq\frac{3}{2}
;

(2)

Using part (a), we then cover the remaining range
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
.

As the details for proving part (a) are essentially the same as that for part (b), we focus on the lower regularity result and thus provide details only for part (b). Namely, in the following, we regard the local well-posedness of (
1.1
) in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for any
s\geq 1
, as in the form of Proposition
5.1
, to have been proven already.

5.1.
A priori bounds

Similar to the setting in
[
51
]
, we do not know that the solutions ensured by Proposition
5.1
are global-in-time.
7
7

7

In the defocusing case, the solutions can be extended globally in time. In the focusing case, we expect global solutions for small initial data but for all large data. Thus, to handle both defocusing/focusing cases simultaneously, we do not assume, in general, that the
H^{1}
solutions exist globally-in-time.

Thus, we first need to obtain a priori estimates for these
H^{1}
-solutions. Let
u_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
be given,
u
be the corresponding solution to (
1.1
) from Proposition
5.1
and let
v
and
w
be defined as in (
3.5
). Then,
we define
\widetilde{u}
and
\widetilde{v}
as in (
3.8
)
and
L_{h}:=\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}
.

Given
0<T\leq 1
,
\frac{1}{2}\leq s\leq 1
,
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{16}
,
b:=\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}
, and
b^{\prime}:=1-b-\delta_{0}=\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}
, we set

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{T}^{s}(u)=\max\Big(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},&\|J^{s}_{x}u\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}},\|v(0)\|_{H^{s}},\|w(0)\|_{H^{s}},\\
&\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}},\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}\Big),\end{split}

(5.1)

where
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{w}
are the nonlinear terms defined in (
3.10
) and (
3.7
).

By the Duhamel formulation of (
3.7
) and (
3.10
) and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v(0)\|_{H^{s}}+T^{\delta_{0}}\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}\lesssim N_{T}^{s}(u),

(5.2)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w(0)\|_{H^{s}}+T^{\delta_{0}}\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}\lesssim N_{T}^{s}(u).

(5.3)

From the smoothness of
u,\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v},
and
w
, the map
T\mapsto N_{T}^{s}(u)
is continuous and non-decreasing (see for instance
[
12
, Remark 3.7]
). It follows from (
3.5
) and Lemma
2.4
that

\displaystyle\|v(0)\|_{H^{s}}+\|w(0)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2})\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{4}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}.

(5.4)

Using the first bounds in (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), Proposition
4.1
, and Lemma
4.2
, we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to 0^{+}}\Big(\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}\Big)=0.

(5.5)

Therefore, from (
5.5
), (
5.2
), (
5.3
), and (
5.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to 0^{+}}N_{T}^{s}(u)\lesssim(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{4}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}.

From (
4.3
) and (
3.14
), we see that

\displaystyle B(u,u)+B(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\lesssim(1+T^{\frac{1}{4}}L_{h}^{2})N_{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}(u)^{2}\big(1+N_{T}^{1/2}(u)^{4}\big).

(5.6)

Then, by Proposition
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
, (
5.6
), (
5.2
)-(
5.3
), and (
3.14
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathcal{N}_{w}(u)\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\theta_{1}}(1+L_{h})Q(N^{1/2}_{T}(u))N_{T}^{s}(u),

(5.7)

where
Q
is some non-negative polynomial of at least degree
1
(in fact, here it is of at least degree
2
) and
\theta_{1}>0
.
Next, combining (
3.15
), (
3.16
), (
5.4
), (
5.2
), (
5.3
), and (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle\max(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}+\|w\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{C}+\big(TM^{3}(1+L_{h})+T^{\frac{1}{4}}+M^{-\theta_{2}}(1+\|\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},b}_{T}})\big)Q(N^{1/2}_{T}(u))N_{T}^{s}(u)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{4}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\big(T(1+L_{h})M^{3}+M^{-\theta_{2}}\big)Q(N^{1/2}_{T}(u))N_{T}^{s}(u)

(5.8)

for some polynomial
Q\geq 1
of degree at least
1
(which may change from line to line), any
0<T\leq 1
, some
\theta_{2}>0
, and for any
M\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large.
Thus, from (
5.4
), (
5.2
)-(
5.3
), (
5.7
), and (
5.8
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{T}^{s}(u)&\leq C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{5}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\\
&\ +C_{2}\big(T^{\theta_{1}}(1+L_{h})M^{3}+M^{-\theta_{2}})Q(N^{1/2}_{T}(u)\big)N_{T}^{s}(u),\end{split}

(5.9)

for some absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
,
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}>0
.
8
8

8

Note that the constants
C_{1},C_{2}
and the polynomial
Q
in (
5.9
) and (
5.11
) depend on
s
here. In the following, we only need to apply (
5.9
) for three regularities:
\frac{1}{2},2
and some fixed
\frac{1}{2}<s\leq\frac{3}{2}
so we may choose the largest of the constants and polynomials.

We now choose
s=\frac{1}{2}
and
M\gg 1
depending on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}
so that

\displaystyle C_{2}M^{-\theta_{2}}Q(4R)<\tfrac{1}{4}\quad\text{where}\quad R:=C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{5}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(5.10)

Then, given this choice of
M
, we choose
T_{\ast}=T_{\ast}(M,L_{h})>0

so that

\displaystyle C_{2}T_{\ast}^{\theta_{1}}M^{3}(1+L_{h})Q(4R)<\tfrac{1}{4}.

(5.11)

By a continuity argument with the choices (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle N_{T}^{1/2}(u)\leq 2R.

(5.12)

for any
0<T\leq T_{\ast}
.
Next, inserting (
5.12
) into (
5.9
) with
s=1
, and reducing
T_{*}=T_{*}(M,L_{h},\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})
if necessary, we find

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{1}_{x}}\leq N^{1}_{T}(u)\leq 2C_{1}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{5}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}.

This a priori bound implies that the maximal time of existence
T_{\max}
for a
C_{T}H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
solution has the uniform lower bound
T_{\max}\geq T_{\ast}
which only depends on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}
and not on
\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T})}
. Finally, from (
5.9
) with
s\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and using (
5.12
), for
0\leq T\leq T_{*}
, we also have that

\displaystyle N_{T}^{s}(u)\leq 2C_{1}(1+\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{5}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}.

(5.13)

Remark 5.2
.

When
\sup_{1\leq h\leq\infty}L_{h}<\infty,

we may choose
T_{\ast}>0
uniformly in
h\geq 1
.

5.2.
Tightness estimates

The goal of this subsection is to establish bounds for the terms
\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}
and
\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}
, which appear in the difference estimates in Lemma
3.6
(ii).

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume that the results of Section
5.1
hold true, with
R
as in (
5.10
) and
T_{*}>0
so that (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) hold for
0\leq T\leq T_{*}
. Then, there exist
\theta_{3}>0
, constants
C_{3}>0
and
C_{4}(R,L_{h})>0
, and
0<T_{\ast\ast}=T_{**}(R,L_{h})\leq T_{\ast}
, such that

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}v(0)\|_{H^{s}}+C_{4}(R,L_{h})\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w(0)\|_{H^{s}}+C_{4}(R,L_{h})\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}},

(5.15)

for all
\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large and
0<T\leq T_{\ast\ast}
.

The proof of Proposition
5.3
requires some preliminary results.

Lemma 5.4
.

Given
0<T\leq 1
, let
u
be a smooth solution to (
1.4
) on
[0,T]
and
\widetilde{u}
be as in (
3.8
).
Define
\widetilde{y}:=e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}:=e^{i\beta{F}[\widetilde{u}]}\widetilde{u}
. Then, for any
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq 1}\|\widetilde{y}\|_{X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}}&\lesssim\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{5})\\
&\quad\times\Big[(1+\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}})\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}})\|J^{s}_{x}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\Big].\end{split}

(5.16)

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
3.5
.
Let
y^{{\dagger}}:=S(-t)[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}]
on
[0,T]
and extend
y^{\dagger}
to
[-1,2]
by setting
\partial_{t}y^{\dagger}=0
on
[-1,2]\setminus[0,T]
. Then,
\eta(t)S(t)y^{{\dagger}}(t)
is an extension of
\widetilde{y}
on
[0,T]
. Then, as in (
3.17
), it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\theta\leq 1}\|\widetilde{y}\|_{X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\partial_{t}+i\partial_{x}^{2})(\widetilde{y})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\widetilde{y}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}.

By (
2.18
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{y}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}.

Next, following the computations in Lemma
3.3
, we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+i\partial_{x}^{2})(\widetilde{y})=&-2\beta e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\\
&+ie^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\big\{\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}\big[\tfrac{\beta}{4\pi}M(\widetilde{u})^{2}-P(\widetilde{u})\big]\widetilde{u}-\beta\widetilde{u}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})+\gamma|\widetilde{u}|^{2}\widetilde{u}\big\}.\end{split}

(5.17)

For the second contribution on the right-hand side of (
5.17
), we argue as in (
3.18
)-(
3.19
) and control it by

\displaystyle T^{\frac{1}{4}}(1+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}})\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{4})\|J^{s}\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}.

For the contribution from the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.17
), we write it as

\displaystyle e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})=\partial_{x}\big(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\big)-\partial_{x}(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u})\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2}).

Then, by (
2.18
) and the fractional Leibniz rule, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}\big(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\big)\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\big[\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\big]\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\Big(\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\|\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\|\widetilde{u}\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{4}(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})\big(\|J^{s}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\big).

Finally, by splitting
\widetilde{u}=(\mathbf{P}_{\ll N}+\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\gtrsim N})\widetilde{u}
, and using (
2.10
)-(
2.11
), we find

\displaystyle\|J_{x}^{s}(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u})\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2})\Big(\|J^{s}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\Big).

It then follows from (
2.5
) that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u})\mathbf{P}_{-}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}(1+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\|J^{s}_{x}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}\widetilde{u}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\Big).

Combining these results then yields (
5.16
).
∎

We now prove a tightness (on the frequency side) result for the trilinear form
y\mapsto\mathbf{P}_{\pm}[y\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|y|^{2})]
in Fourier restriction norm spaces. In particular, we exploit a nonlinear smoothing effect for some parts of this contribution.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
0<T\leq 1
,
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}-2\delta
,
b=\frac{1}{2}+\delta
, for small
0<\delta<1
, and
y\in X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}
for every
0\leq\theta\leq 1
and such that
\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}y\in X^{s,b}_{T}
or
\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}y\in X^{s,b}_{T}
. Then, for
\mathcal{Q}_{h}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}
, then for all
\Lambda\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm}\Pi_{>\Lambda}\big[y\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|y|^{2})\big]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}&\lesssim\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{10}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}\|y\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}^{2}\Big[\|y\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}_{T}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}y\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}\Big]\\
&\qquad+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}\|y\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{\pm}y\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}},\end{split}

(5.18)

where
\|y\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}_{T}}:=\max_{0\leq\theta\leq 1}\|y\|_{X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}}.

Proof.

We begin by writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{x}\{y\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|y|^{2})\}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}}\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi_{3}-\xi_{2})\widehat{y}(\xi_{1})\overline{\widehat{y}(\xi_{2})}\widehat{y}(\xi_{3})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(0)M(y)\widehat{y}(\xi)+\mathcal{F}_{x}\{\mathcal{N}_{h}(y)+\mathcal{R}_{1}(y)+\mathcal{R}_{2}(y)\}(\xi)

(5.19)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\{\mathcal{N}_{h}(y)\}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}\\
\xi_{1},\xi_{3}\neq\xi\end{subarray}}\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi_{3}-\xi_{2})\widehat{y}(\xi_{1})\overline{\widehat{y}(\xi_{2})}\widehat{y}(\xi_{3}),

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\{\mathcal{R}_{1}(y)\}(\xi):=\widehat{y}(\xi)\sum_{\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi-\xi^{\prime})|\widehat{y}(\xi^{\prime})|^{2},\quad\mathcal{F}\{\mathcal{R}_{2}(y)\}(\xi):=\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(0)|\widehat{y}(\xi)|^{2}\widehat{y}(\xi),

and proceed to estimate the contributions from each piece separately.
Notice that
\|\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi)\|_{\ell^{\infty}_{\xi}}=\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{\text{op}}
.
From (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm}\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathcal{R}_{2}(y)\|_{X^{s,b^{\prime}}_{T}}\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm}\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathcal{R}_{2}(y)\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}\|y\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{\pm}y\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}.

A similar bound holds for the contribution from
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, by placing
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi)
into
\ell^{\infty}_{\xi}
.
Similarly, the first term in (
5.19
) is controlled by the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.18
).

Thus, it remains to control the contribution in (
5.20
), which is the main difficulty.
Let
y^{{\dagger}}
be an arbitrary extension of
y
on
[0,T]
. We define the following set

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\Lambda,\pm}:=\{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\,:

\displaystyle\,\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{3}-\xi_{4}=0,

\displaystyle\xi_{1},\xi_{3}\neq\xi_{4},\,\,\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})=\pm 1,\,\,|\xi_{4}|>\Lambda\},

and
\sigma_{\ell}:=|\tau_{\ell}-\xi_{\ell}^{2}|
,
\ell=1,\ldots,4
, to be the modulations. Now, by duality, it suffices to control

\displaystyle\intt_{\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}-\tau_{4}=0}\sum_{\Gamma_{\Lambda,\pm}}\langle\xi_{4}\rangle^{s}\widehat{\mathcal{Q}_{h}}(\xi_{3}-\xi_{2})\frac{\widehat{g}(\tau_{4},\xi_{4})}{\langle\sigma_{4}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\widehat{y^{{\dagger}}}(\tau_{1},\xi_{1})\overline{\widehat{y^{{\dagger}}}(\tau_{2},\xi_{2})}\widehat{y^{{\dagger}}}(\tau_{3},\xi_{3})d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3}

for
g\in L^{2}_{t,x}
. We dyadically decompose the spatial frequencies of all of the functions and use the boundedness of
\mathcal{Q}_{h}(\xi)
to further reduce to controlling

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}\\
N_{4}\gtrsim\Lambda\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\intt_{\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}-\tau_{4}=0}\sum_{\Gamma_{\Lambda,\pm}}\langle\xi_{4}\rangle^{s}\frac{|\widehat{\mathbf{P}_{N_{4}}g}(\tau_{4},\xi_{4})|}{\langle\sigma_{4}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\prod_{j=1}^{3}|\widehat{\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}y^{{\dagger}}}(\tau_{j},\xi_{j})|d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3}=:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}\\
N_{4}\gtrsim\Lambda\end{subarray}}I_{\overline{N}},

where
\overline{N}=(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4})
.
By symmetry, we may assume that
N_{1}\geq N_{3}
,
we let
N_{\max}\geq N_{\med}\geq N_{\min}
be the decreasing rearrangement of
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
,
and

\sigma_{\MAX}=\max_{j=1,\ldots,4}|\sigma_{j}|
. Then, the phase function satisfies

\displaystyle|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|=|\xi_{1}^{2}-\xi_{2}^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2}-\xi^{2}|\sim|\xi_{1}-\xi_{4}||\xi_{3}-\xi_{4}|\lesssim\sigma_{\MAX}.

(5.21)

We split the summation into a number of cases.

\bullet

Case 1:

|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\gtrsim N_{\max}N_{4}

Subcase 1.1:

N_{\max}\gg N_{\med}

Here, we have
N_{\max}\sim N_{4}
.
If
N_{\max}=N_{1}
, then
\textup{sgn}(\xi_{1})=\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})
and we can place
\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}
for free into the factor with
\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}
. Then,
from Parseval’s theorem, Hölder’s inequality, (
3.2
), and Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}\bigg\|{\mathcal{F}}_{t,x}^{-1}\bigg(\frac{|\widehat{\mathbf{P}_{N_{4}}g}|}{\langle\sigma_{4}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\bigg)\bigg\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}y^{\dagger}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}y^{\dagger}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}\|g\|_{X^{0,-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}\prod_{j=2}^{3}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0,\frac{3}{8}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}(N_{\max}N_{4})^{-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}N_{1}^{-s}N_{\min}^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\text{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{4}+4\delta}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\text{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|y^{\dagger}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

where the second factor in the third inequality comes from gaining a power of
|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|^{-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}
by considering the different choices of
\sigma_{\MAX}
. Using the negative power of
N_{\max}
, we can sum in the dyadics and gain a power of
\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{10}}
for
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

Next, consider
N_{\max}=N_{2}
. If
\sigma_{\MAX}=|\sigma_{4}|
, we place factors with frequencies comparable to
(N_{4},N_{\med},N_{\max},N_{\min})

in
(L^{2},L^{4},L^{4},L^{\infty})
, together with (
3.2
), Bernstein’s inequality, and (
3.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}(N_{\max}N_{4})^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}N_{\med}^{-\frac{1}{8}}N_{\max}^{-s+\frac{3}{8}}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0-,\frac{1}{2}+}}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{4}+4\delta+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}}\|y^{\dagger}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(5.24)

Now let
\sigma_{\max},\sigma_{\med},\sigma_{\min}
denote the modulations of the factors associated with frequencies around
N_{\max},N_{\med},N_{\min}
, respectively.
If
\sigma_{\MAX}=|\sigma_{\med}|
, we instead place the factors
in
(L^{4},L^{2},L^{4},L^{\infty})
:

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}N_{\med}^{\frac{1}{2}+}(N_{\max}N_{4})^{-1}N_{\max}^{-s+\frac{3}{8}}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-1,1}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0-,\frac{1}{2}+}}

(5.25)

which is controlled by (
5.24
) for small
\delta
.
If
\sigma_{\MAX}=|\sigma_{\min}|
, we exchange the roles of the second and fourth factors in (
5.25
).
Lastly, if
\sigma_{\MAX}=|\sigma_{\max}|
, we place the factors in
(L^{4},L^{4},L^{2},L^{\infty})
:

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}N_{\med}^{-\frac{1}{8}}N_{\max}^{-s+1}(N_{\max}N_{4})^{-1}N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s-1,1}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{0-,\frac{1}{2}+}},

(5.26)

which is also controlled by (
5.24
).

Subcase 1.2:

N_{\max}\sim N_{\med}\gtrsim N_{4}

In this region, we proceed as in the part of Subcase 1.1 where
N_{\max}=N_{2}
,
applying the estimates in (
5.23
), (
5.25
), and (
5.26
) to obtain

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(N_{4}^{s-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}N_{\max}^{-s-\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}+N_{4}^{s-1}N_{\max}^{-s-\frac{1}{8}+}\big)N_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{8}+4\delta+}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2},

and the estimate follows for
\delta>0
small.

\bullet

Case 2:

|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\ll N_{\max}N_{4}

Subcase 2.1:

N_{\max}\gg N_{\med}

Here,
N_{4}\sim N_{\max}
.
If
N_{\max}=N_{1}
, then
N_{1}\sim N_{4}\gg N_{2},N_{3}
, and thus
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}|\sim N_{1}
. From the assumption on the phase and the restriction to
\Gamma_{\Lambda,\pm}
, this implies that
1\leq|\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}|=|\xi_{1}-\xi_{4}|\ll N_{4}\sim N_{1}
and
\textup{sgn}(\xi_{1})=\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})
, allowing us to add
\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}
to
\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}
.
Thus, we have
|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\gtrsim|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}|\sim N_{1}
,
and we proceed as in (
5.22
),
but gaining only
N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}
from the phase:

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}(N_{\max})^{-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}N_{\max}^{-s}N_{\min}^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|y^{\dagger}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

which gives a negative power of the maximum frequency, as intended.

The case
N_{\max}=N_{2}\sim N_{4}\gg N_{1}\geq N_{3}
cannot happen, since
|\Phi(\overline{\xi})|\sim|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}||\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}|\sim N_{2}^{2}\sim N_{\max}N_{4}
which contradicts the phase assumption.

Subcase 2.2:

N_{\max}\sim N_{\med}\gtrsim N_{4}

Since
N_{1}\geq N_{3}
, we have
N_{1}\sim N_{\max}\sim N_{\med}\gg N_{4}
.
From the factorization in (
5.21
), if
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{4}|\gtrsim N_{1}
, then
|\xi_{3}-\xi_{4}|\ll N_{4}\sim N_{3}
and hence
\textup{sgn}(\xi_{3})=\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})
allowing us to add
\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}
to the
\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}
contribution.
Similarly, if
|\xi_{3}-\xi_{4}|\gtrsim N_{1}
, then
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{4}|\ll N_{4}\sim N_{1}
and
\textup{sgn}(\xi_{1})=\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})
, allowing us to add
\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}
to the
\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}
term instead. In both cases, proceeding as in (
5.22
), gaining only
N_{\max}
from the phase and
N_{j}^{-s}\sim N_{4}^{-s}
from the term
\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}y^{\dagger}
,
j=1,3
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{4}^{s}(N_{\max})^{-\frac{1}{8}+2\delta}N_{j}^{-s}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\text{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|y^{\dagger}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

and the estimate follows.

It remains to consider
|\xi_{1}-\xi_{4}|,|\xi_{3}-\xi_{4}|\ll N_{1}
, which implies
N_{1}\sim N_{2}\sim N_{3}\sim N_{4}
and
\textup{sgn}(\xi_{1})=\textup{sgn}(\xi_{4})
. Then, we proceed as in (
5.22
), with no gain from the phase function:

\displaystyle I_{\overline{N}}\lesssim N_{4}^{s}N_{1}^{-s}N_{\min}^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\pm,\textup{hi}}y^{\dagger}\|_{X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|y^{\dagger}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2},

which again gives a negative power of the maximum frequency, completing the estimate for
\mathcal{N}_{h}
in (
5.20
), and hence also that of (
5.18
).
∎

With the two auxiliary estimates in Lemmas
5.4
-
5.5
, we now show tightness for
\widetilde{v}
and
w
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

Let
0\leq T\leq T_{**}\leq T_{*}
, with
T_{**}
to be chosen later.
We begin with (
5.14
).
From the Duhamel formula for (
3.10
), the linear estimates in Lemma
3.1
, and the conservation of
M(\widetilde{u})
and
P(\widetilde{u})
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}(0)\|_{H^{s}}+C_{0}T^{\delta_{0}}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{+}[\widetilde{v}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}

\displaystyle+C_{0}T^{\delta_{0}}\max_{\mathcal{Q}_{h}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+}\Pi_{>\Lambda}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}

\displaystyle+C_{0}T^{\delta_{0}}\|\widetilde{u}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\big(1+\|\widetilde{u}(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}.

By reducing
T_{**}
, if necessary, so that
C_{0}T_{**}^{\delta_{0}}4R^{2}(1+4R^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}
, it follows from (
5.12
) and (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}(0)\|_{H^{s}}+2C_{0}T^{\delta_{0}}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{+}[\widetilde{v}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}

\displaystyle+2C_{0}T^{\delta_{0}}\max_{\mathcal{Q}_{h}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+}\Pi_{>\Lambda}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}.

In view of the signs of the frequencies, we have the (key) identity

\displaystyle\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{+}[\widetilde{v}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]=\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{+}[\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})].

It then follows from Proposition
4.1
,
(
3.14
) in Lemma
3.5
, and (
5.12
)
that

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{+}[\widetilde{v}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,b^{\prime}}_{T}}\leq C(R)\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}.

Thus, by again reducing
T_{**}
if necessary, we have

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}\leq 4C_{0}\Big(\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}(0)\|_{H^{s}}+T^{\delta_{0}}\max_{\mathcal{Q}_{h}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}}\|\mathbf{P}_{+}\Pi_{>\Lambda}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}\Big).

(5.27)

It remains to control the last on the right-hand side of (
5.27
). We write

\displaystyle e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})=\widetilde{y}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{y}|^{2}),

where
\widetilde{y}:=e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}
. It then follows from Lemma
5.5
, Lemma
5.4
, (
5.12
), and (
5.13
), that

\displaystyle\max_{\mathcal{Q}_{h}\in\{\textup{Id},\mathcal{G}_{h}\}}

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{+}\Pi_{>\Lambda}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F}}\widetilde{u}\mathcal{Q}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}|^{2})]\|_{X^{s,-b^{\prime}}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+L_{h})\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{10}}\|\widetilde{y}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}^{2}\big[\|\widetilde{y}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s}_{T}}+\|\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}\big]+C(1+L_{h})T^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{2}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq C(R,L_{h})\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{10}}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}+C(R,L_{h})T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}},

recalling that
L_{h}:=\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}

Inserting this into (
5.27
) and reducing
T_{**}
further, if necessary, then establishes (
5.14
).

The estimate for (
5.15
) is done similarly, using the Duhamel formula for
w
based on (
3.7
). We note again the key identity

\displaystyle\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{-}[w\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})]=\Pi_{>\Lambda}\mathbf{P}_{-}[\Pi_{>\Lambda}w\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})],

which, combined with Proposition
4.1
, allows us to handle the contribution from the main term in (
3.7
), as we did for the main term in the equation for
\widetilde{v}
above. The contribution from the remaining nonlinear terms in (
3.7
) are handled by using Lemma
5.5
(with
y=u
) and recalling that
\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}u=w
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

5.3.
Difference estimates

In this subsection, our aim is to control the difference of solutions and their corresponding gauge variables in suitable norms.
Before stating the main result of this subsection (Lemma
5.6
), we introduce some notations, for
\frac{1}{2}\leq s\leq 1
,
b=\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}
, and
b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}-2\delta_{0}
, as before:

\displaystyle\|f\|_{S^{s}(T)}

\displaystyle:=\|J^{s}_{x}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}f\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},

(5.28)

\displaystyle\|({\bf{U}},{\bf{\widetilde{U}}},{\bf{\widetilde{V}}},{\bf{W}})\|_{R^{s}(T)}

\displaystyle:=\|{\bf U}\|_{S^{s}(T)}+\|{\bf\widetilde{U}}\|_{S^{s}(T)}+\|{\bf\widetilde{V}}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}+\|{\bf W}\|_{X^{s,b}_{T}}.

(5.29)

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq s\leq 1
,
u_{j}
,
j=1,2
be two solutions to (
1.1
) with initial data
u_{j}(0)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
,
j=1,2
, which exist at least on the same time interval
[0,T_{0}]
for
T_{0}>0
only depending on
L_{h}:=\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{L^{4}\to L^{4}}
and

\displaystyle K_{s}:=\max_{j}\|u_{j}(0)\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})},

(5.30)

with
K:=K_{\frac{1}{2}}
.
Let
F_{j}=F_{j}[u_{j}]
and
(v_{j},w_{j})
be as in (
3.4
) and (
3.5
), respectively, and
(\widetilde{u}_{j},\widetilde{v}_{j})
as in (
3.8
),
j=1,2
.
Also, define the differences

U:=u_{1}-u_{2}
,
\widetilde{U}=\widetilde{u}_{1}-\widetilde{u}_{2}
,
\widetilde{V}:=\widetilde{v}_{1}-\widetilde{v}_{2}
, and
W:=w_{1}-w_{2}
, then the following estimates hold

\displaystyle\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big(1+K+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)\big)\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle+C(K)(1+L_{h})(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[K\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big],

(5.31)

\displaystyle\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{s}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)K_{s}(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle+C(K)(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[K\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s}}\big]

\displaystyle+C(K)\big[T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}+K_{s}\big]\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)},

(5.32)

for some constants
C(K),C_{0}(K)>0
depending only on
K
,
\Lambda,M>0
sufficiently large, and for
0\leq T\leq T_{0}\leq 1
sufficiently small, where
T_{0}=T_{0}(K,L_{h})
for (
5.31
) and
T_{0}=T_{0}(K_{s},L_{h},M)
for (
5.32
).

Before proceeding to the proof, we prove some additional estimates involving differences of
F_{j}
and of
e^{i\beta F_{j}}
. In particular, (
5.34
) is crucial in proving (
5.31
) at the endpoint
s=\frac{1}{2}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
0\leq T\leq 1
,
u_{j}
be smooth solutions to (
1.1
),
\widetilde{u}_{j},F_{j},\widetilde{F}_{j},U,\widetilde{U},K
be as in Lemma
5.50
. Then, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|u_{1}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|u_{2}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}})\|U\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}},

(5.33)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F}_{1}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F}_{2}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim K\|{U}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+C(K)\big(M^{-\theta_{4}}+TM^{\frac{3}{2}}\big)\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}},

(5.34)

for some
\theta_{4}>0
and
C(K)>0
only depending on
K
. Also, (
5.33
)-(
5.34
) hold when exchanging
(\widetilde{u}_{j},\widetilde{F}_{j},\widetilde{U})
with
(u_{j},F_{j},U)
.

Proof.

We omit details of the proof of (
5.33
), as it follows from (
3.4
) and Bernstein’s inequality; see (
2.15
), for example.

To show (
5.34
), we first write

\displaystyle e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}=-i\beta(\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}})\int_{0}^{1}e^{-i\beta(\mu\widetilde{F_{1}}+(1-\mu)\widetilde{F_{2}})}d\mu=:(\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}})G(\widetilde{F_{1}},\widetilde{F_{2}}),

and decompose the relevant term as follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}\big[\mathbf{P}_{>2^{-10}M}(\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}})G(\widetilde{F_{1}},\widetilde{F_{2}})\big]

(5.35)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}\big[\mathbf{P}_{\leq 2^{-10}M}(\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{>2^{-10}M}G(\widetilde{F_{1}},\widetilde{F_{2}})\big]

(5.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}\big[\mathbf{P}_{\leq 2^{-10}M}(\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}})\mathbf{P}_{\leq 2^{-10}M}G(\widetilde{F_{1}},\widetilde{F_{2}})\big].

(5.37)

From (
2.15
), we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{expdiffM1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\lesssim M^{-1}\|\mathbf{P}_{>2^{-10}M}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2}-|\widetilde{u}_{2}|^{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\lesssim M^{-1+}K\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

For the second contribution, it follows from (
5.33
) and (
2.10
) that

\displaystyle\|\eqref{expdiffM2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widetilde{F_{1}}-\widetilde{F_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{>2^{-10}M}G\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim K\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\Big(\max_{j=1,2}\sup_{0\leq\mu\leq 1}\|\mathbf{P}_{>2^{-20}M}e^{-i\beta\mu\widetilde{F}_{j}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{-1+}K^{3}\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

As for the main term (
5.37
), by the fundamental theorem of calculus in time, (
5.33
), (
3.11
), and (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\|\eqref{expdiffM3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\|\widetilde{U}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+CTM^{\frac{3}{2}+}\|\partial_{t}\widetilde{F}_{1}-\partial_{t}\widetilde{F}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{-1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\|\widetilde{U}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+C(K)TM^{\frac{3}{2}+}\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

This proves (
5.34
). The only difference for the ungauged variables
e^{-i\beta F_{j}}
is that there is one fewer (harmless) term in
\partial_{t}F_{j}
as compared to
\partial_{t}\widetilde{F}_{j}
,
j=1,2
. We omit the similar details.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Let
s_{1}\in\{\frac{1}{2},s\}
.
We first recall a priori bounds for
u_{j},\widetilde{v}_{j},w_{j}
,
j=1,2
.
By the results of Subsection
5.1
, namely (
5.12
), (
5.10
), and (
5.13
), we have the bounds
