\displaystyle\begin{split}\max\big(\|u_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{*}}H^{s_{1}}_{x}},\|J^{s_{1}}_{x}u_{j}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T_{*},x}}}\big)&\leq 2C_{1}(1+\|u_{j}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})^{5}\|u_{j}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}\leq Q_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}},\end{split}

(5.38)

for
j=1,2
,
T_{\ast}>0
is as in (
5.11
), and
Q_{0}(x)=2C_{1}(1+x)^{5}
.
Then,
from (
5.2
)-(
5.3
) and (
5.38
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\max_{j=1,2}\big\{\|\widetilde{v}_{j}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|w_{j}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}\big\}&\lesssim\max_{j=1,2}N_{T}^{s_{1}}(u_{j})\leq Q_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}},\end{split}

(5.39)

for any
0<T\leq T_{\ast}
.

We start by estimating the
\widetilde{V}
and
W
contributions in the
R^{s_{1}}(T)
-norm in (
5.29
).
At time
t=0
, from (
3.8
), (
1.14
), and (
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}(0)=V(0)=\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}\big[(e^{i\beta F_{1}}-e^{i\beta F_{2}})u_{1}\big](0)+\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}\big[e^{i\beta F_{2}}U\big](0)\quad\text{and}\quad W(0)=\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}U(0).

Then, from (
2.19
), (
2.18
), (
5.38
), the mean value theorem, and (
5.33
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widetilde{V}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}&\lesssim Q(K)\big[K\|{F_{1}}(0)-{F_{2}}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|{U}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}+(1+K_{s_{1}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big]\\
&\lesssim Q(K)\big[\|{U}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}+(1+K_{s_{1}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big],\\
\|W(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}&\lesssim\|U(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}},\end{split}

(5.40)

for some polynomial
Q\geq 0
.
Next, from (
3.10
) and (
3.7
), we obtain equations for
\widetilde{V}
and
W
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{V}+i\partial_{x}^{2}\widetilde{V}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}_{1}}(\widetilde{u}_{2})-\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{v}_{2}}(\widetilde{u}_{2})

\displaystyle=-2\beta\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\widetilde{V}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2})]-2\beta\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\widetilde{v}_{2}\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2}-|\widetilde{u}_{2}|^{2})]

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+i\gamma\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}})|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2}\widetilde{u}_{1}]+i\gamma\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2}\widetilde{u_{1}}-|\widetilde{u}_{2}|^{2}\widetilde{u}_{2})]

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}-i\beta\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[(e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}})\widetilde{u}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2})]-i\beta\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[e^{i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}(\widetilde{u}_{1}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}_{1}|^{2})-\widetilde{u_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|\widetilde{u}_{2}|^{2}))]

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}+i\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}\big[\tfrac{\beta}{4\pi}\big(M(\widetilde{u}_{1})^{2}-M(\widetilde{u}_{2})^{2}\big)-P(\widetilde{u}_{1})+P(\widetilde{u}_{2})\big]\widetilde{v}_{1}+i\tfrac{\beta}{\pi}\big[\tfrac{\beta}{4\pi}M(\widetilde{u}_{2})^{2}-P(\widetilde{u}_{2})\big]\widetilde{V}

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j=1}^{8}\mathcal{A}_{j},

(5.41)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W+i\partial_{x}^{2}W

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{w_{1}}(u_{1})-\mathcal{N}_{w_{2}}(u_{2})

\displaystyle=2\beta\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}[W\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{1}|^{2})]+2\beta\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}[w_{2}\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{1}|^{2}-|u_{2}|^{2})]

\displaystyle\hphantom{XX}-i\beta\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}[u_{1}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{1}|^{2})-u_{2}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{2}|^{2})]+i\gamma\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}[|u_{1}|^{2}u_{1}-|u_{2}|^{2}u_{2}]

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{B}_{j}.

(5.42)

We now estimate these terms using the Duhamel formulation of these equations and Lemma
3.1
.
From Proposition
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathcal{A}_{j}\|_{X^{s_{1},-b^{\prime}}_{T}}&\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\Big(B(\widetilde{u}_{1},\widetilde{u}_{1})\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\widetilde{v}_{2}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}[B(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{u}_{2})+B(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{u}_{2})]\Big),\\
\max_{j=1,2}\|\mathcal{B}_{j}\|_{X^{s_{1},-b^{\prime}}_{T}}&\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}\Big(B(u_{1},u_{1})\|W\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|w_{2}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}[B(U,u_{1})+B(U,u_{2})]\Big),\end{split}

(5.43)

for

), (

), (

\max_{j=3,4}\|\mathcal{B}_{j}\|_{X^{s_{1},-b^{\prime}}_{T}}&\lesssim T^{\delta_{0}}(1+L_{h})C_{0}(K)[K_{s_{1}}\|U\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+K\|U\|_{S^{s_{1}}(T)}\big],\end{split}

(5.44)

for some

depending only on

.
Using the conservation of mass and momentum, and (

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{7}+\mathcal{A}_{8}\|_{X^{s_{1},-b^{\prime}}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq T^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{0}(K)\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}Q_{0}(K)^{2}\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}.

(5.45)

Combining (
5.43
), (
5.44
), (
5.45
), and recalling (

), (

), (

),

&+C_{0}(K)T^{\theta}(1+L_{h})\Big\{\|U\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}+\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{s_{1}}(T)}\\
&\hskip 99.58464pt+K_{s_{1}}(\|U\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)})\Big\},\end{split}

(5.46)

for some

.

We now proceed to estimating the
\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}
- and

-norms of

in (

).
From (

), and (

(5.47)

for any

.

Combining (

), (

), (

), (

), with (

), (

\displaystyle\|U\|_{S^{s_{1}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}

\displaystyle+(T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K)+1)\big[\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|W\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{2}(K)\big[T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}(1+L_{h})+T^{\frac{1}{8}}(1+K_{s_{1}})\big]\|U\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle+M^{-\theta}C_{2}(K)\big\{\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|U\|_{S^{s_{1}}(T)}\big\},

(5.48)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{s_{1}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}})\|U(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}

\displaystyle+(T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K)+1)\big[\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|W\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{2}(K)\big[T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}(1+L_{h})+T^{\frac{1}{8}}(1+K_{s_{1}})\big]\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}w_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle+M^{-\theta}C_{2}(K)\big\{\|\widetilde{V}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{s_{1}}(T)}\big\}.

(5.49)

Then, putting (
5.48
), (
5.49
), (

), (

),together, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{s_{1}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\big(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)+C_{2}(K)K_{s_{1}}\big)\|U(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}

\displaystyle+(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}\big]

\displaystyle+C_{2}(K)\big\{T^{\theta}(1+L_{h})+M^{-\theta}\big\}\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{s_{1}}(T)}

\displaystyle+C_{2}(K)(1+L_{h})\big[T^{\theta}K_{s_{1}}+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}\big]\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{1}}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{2}}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta F_{1}}-e^{-i\beta F_{2}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}w_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}.

(5.50)

By choosing

small enough and

Next, we estimate the fifth and sixth terms on the right-hand side of (

Note that from (

), and (

\displaystyle\|J_{x}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}[e^{i\beta F_{1}(0)}u_{1}(0)]\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{4}}(1+\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}})^{2}\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{x}}.

(5.51)

Then by the Duhamel formula for

and

, Lemma

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}\widetilde{v}_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{x}}+M^{-\frac{1}{4}}C_{3}(K)K+T^{\delta_{0}}(1+L_{h})Q_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}},

(5.52)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}w_{1}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{x}}+T^{\delta_{0}}Q_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}}.

(5.53)

If
s>\frac{1}{2}
, note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim M^{-(s-\frac{1}{2})}\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s}}.

(5.54)

Then, we start by using (
5.50
) when
s_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
, which is then used to obtain the estimate for
s_{1}=s
. In particular, for
s_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
, combining (

and large

), (

), mean value theorem, (

, and (

), we get

\displaystyle\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{1}\big(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)+C_{2}(K)K\big)\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle+2(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}C_{2}(K)+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big]

\displaystyle+2C_{2}(K)(1+L_{h})\big[T^{\theta}K+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}+M^{-(s-\frac{1}{2})}\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s}}\big]\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)},

where we take
M\geq M_{0}=M_{0}(K,L_{h},\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s}})\gg 1
potentially larger so that

C_{2}(K)(1+L_{h})M^{-(s-\frac{1}{2})}\|u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{s}}<\frac{1}{100}
and
0<T\leq T_{0}=T_{0}(K,L_{h},M)\leq 1
possibly smaller to guarantee that

C_{2}(K)(1+L_{h})[T^{\theta}K+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}]<\tfrac{1}{100}
, to hide the last contribution on the left-hand side:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}&\leq 4C_{1}\big(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)+C_{2}(K)K\big)\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&+4(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}C_{2}(K)+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{1}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big]\end{split}

for any
0<T\leq T_{0}=T_{0}(L_{h},K_{s})
. Then, for the same choice of
0\leq T\leq T_{0}
and
M\geq M_{0}\gg 1
, combining (
5.50
) for
s_{1}=s
with Proposition
5.3
, and (
5.51
) gives (
5.32
).

It remains to show the estimate at the endpoint
s=\frac{1}{2}
, namely (
5.31
), where we cannot use (
5.54
), and thus need a more careful argument to avoid picking
T_{0}
depending on the profile of
u_{1}(0)
instead of only its norm. Using (
5.52
), (
5.53
), and (
5.34
), we control the fifth and sixth terms in (
5.50
) by

\displaystyle CK\big(\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F}_{1}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F}_{2}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta{F}_{1}}-e^{-i\beta{F}_{2}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+CT^{\delta_{0}}(1+L_{h})Q_{0}(K)K^{2}\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|U\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq CK^{2}\|U(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+C^{\prime}(K)\big\{M^{-\theta_{4}}+TM^{\frac{3}{2}}+T^{\delta_{0}}(1+L_{h})\big\}\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|U\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\big).

Replacing the above estimate in (
5.50
), together with Proposition
5.3
, (
5.51
), and taking
M\geq M_{0}=M_{0}(K,L_{h})\gg 1
potentially larger and
0<T\leq T_{0}=T_{0}(K,L_{h},M)\leq 1
smaller, to hide the terms depending on
\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}
on the left-hand side, completing the proof of (
5.31
).
∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

Now that we have (
5.31
) and (
5.32
) in Lemma
5.6
, the local well-posedness of (
1.1
) follows from a modification of a standard argument. See
[
50
, Section 4C]
, for example.
Given
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
, we consider the sequence of smooth initial data
\{u_{0,j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
defined by
u_{0,j}=\mathbf{P}_{\leq j}u_{0}.

Then,
\{u_{0,j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
belongs to
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
and by Proposition
5.1
, the associated sequence of solutions
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
to (
1.1
) belong to
C([0,T_{\ast}];H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}))
, for some
T_{*}=T_{*}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}})>0
.
From Subsection
5.1
and (
5.13
), we then have that
N^{s}_{T_{\ast}}(u_{j})\leq Q_{0}(K)K_{s}
, uniformly in
j
, where
Q_{0}(x)=(1+x)^{5}
and
K
is as in (
5.30
).
Note that in place of (
5.54
), we may choose
M
uniformly in
j
since

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{0,j}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\lesssim M^{-(s-\frac{1}{2})}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}.

Then, from Lemma
5.6
, there exists
0<T_{0}\leq T_{\ast}
such that both (
5.31
) and (
5.32
) hold for
0<T\leq T_{0}
, where all constants are independent of
j
.

Let
\widetilde{u}_{j},\widetilde{v}_{j}
be the gauge variables in (
3.8
) associated with
u_{j}
, and
{v}_{j},w_{j}
as in (
3.5
). It follows from (
5.31
) that, for
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(u_{j}-u_{j^{\prime}},\widetilde{u}_{j}-\widetilde{u}_{j^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{j}-\widetilde{v}_{j^{\prime}},w_{j}-w_{j^{\prime}})\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big(1+K+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)\big)\|(u_{j}-u_{j^{\prime}})(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K)(1+L_{h})(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[K\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big].

Thus, taking limits in
j,j^{\prime}
, followed by taking
\Lambda\to\infty
, allows us to
conclude that
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T_{0})
,
while
\{\widetilde{v}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}},\{w_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
are Cauchy in
X^{\frac{1}{2},b}_{T_{0}}
.
Consequently,
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to a limit
u\in S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T_{0})
and
\{\widetilde{v}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{w_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
converge to limits
\widetilde{v}
and
w
in
X^{\frac{1}{2},b}_{T_{0}}
, respectively.
By using this convergence property in
R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)
in (
5.32
), by a similar argument, we upgrade the convergence of
\{u_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}},\{\widetilde{v}_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}},\{w_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
to
R^{s}(T)
.
Moreover, a computation shows that
u
is a distributional solution to (
1.1
), with
w=\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}u
and
v=\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[e^{i\beta F[u]}u]
.

We now move onto the uniqueness property of these solutions, as in Theorem
1.1
(i).
Let
\underline{u}
be another solution to (
1.1
) on
[0,T]
, for some
T>0
, with
\underline{u}|_{t=0}=u_{0}
and belonging to the same class as
u
in (
1.7
). We then define
K
as in (
5.30
) and, by reducing
T
if necessary, we have that
u
and
\underline{u}
satisfy (
5.38
).
We define the gauged variables
\widetilde{\underline{v}}:=\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}[e^{i\beta F[\underline{u}]}\underline{u}]
and
\underline{w}=\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}\underline{u}
.
By (
5.4
),

\displaystyle\|\underline{v}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\underline{w}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq Q_{0}(K).

By the dominated convergence theorem, we can find
N>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{>N}\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\widetilde{v}}}(\widetilde{\underline{u}})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},-b^{\prime}}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{>N}\mathcal{N}_{\underline{w}}(\underline{u})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},-b^{\prime}}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}Q_{0}(K).

Also, from (
4.19
), (
3.7
), (
3.10
), (
2.6
) and similar computations as in Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\widetilde{v}}}(\underline{u})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},-b^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}\mathcal{N}_{\underline{w}}(\underline{u})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},-b^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\theta}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\widetilde{v}}}(\widetilde{\underline{u}})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H_{x}^{-1}}+\|\mathcal{N}_{\underline{w}}(\underline{u})\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{-1}_{x}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\theta}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|\widetilde{\underline{v}}\|_{L^{2}_{T}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}})\lesssim T^{\theta}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|\widetilde{\underline{v}}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2},0}_{T}}).

Then, by reducing
T>0
if necessary, it follows that
N_{T}^{1/2}(\underline{u})\leq 2R
, with
R
as in (
5.10
), thus satisfying (
5.12
). Then, we may apply (
5.31
) to
(u-\underline{u},\widetilde{u}-\widetilde{\underline{u}},\widetilde{v}-\widetilde{\underline{v}},w-\underline{w})
and take
\Lambda\to\infty
to see that
\underline{u}\equiv u
on
[0,T]
. This establishes the uniqueness claim (iterating in time, if necessary).

As for the continuous dependence, we can follow a similar argument as in
[
50
, Section 4C]
relying on (
5.31
) and (
5.32
). We point out that the choices of
\Lambda
and
M
can be made uniformly over any convergent approximating sequence of initial data.

6.
The infinite depth limit

In this section, we establish the local-in-time convergence of solutions to (
1.1
) to solutions to (
1.6
) as
h\to\infty
.
We focus on the low regularity setting
\frac{1}{2}\leq s\leq 1
. The convergence in the remaining range
1<s\leq\frac{3}{2}
follows from the same ideas and the estimates already appearing earlier. Fix
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
and let
\{u_{0,h}\}_{1\leq h<\infty}
be a net converging to
u_{0}
in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
as
h\to\infty
.

By Theorem
1.1
, and in view of Remark
5.2
, there exists
T_{\ast}=T_{\ast}(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}})>0
such that we have a unique solution
u_{\infty}
to (
1.6
) with
u_{\infty}|_{t=0}=u_{0}
and, for each
1\leq h<\infty
, a unique solution
u_{h}
to (
1.1
) with
u_{h}|_{t=0}=u_{0,h}
on
[0,T_{\ast}]
.
We then define
F_{\infty}:=F[u_{\infty}]
and
F_{h}:=F[u_{h}]
, where
F
is the primitive defined in (
3.4
), and the gauged variables
(v_{\infty},w_{\infty}),(v_{h},w_{h})
as in (
3.5
) and
\widetilde{v}_{\infty},\widetilde{v}_{h}
as in (
3.8
).
For
(u_{\infty},\widetilde{v}_{\infty},w_{\infty})
and
(u_{h},\widetilde{v}_{h},w_{h})
, we define
N_{T}^{s}
as in (
5.1
).
As
u_{0,h}
converges to
u_{0}
, there exists
h_{0}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{h\geq h_{0}}\|u_{0,h}\|_{H^{s}}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+1=:K_{s}.

Then, by repeating the arguments of Subsection
5.1
(noting that
T_{\ast}
can be chosen uniformly in
h_{0}\leq h\leq\infty
), we have

\displaystyle N^{s_{1}}_{T}(u_{h})\leq 2^{7}Q_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}}=:\widetilde{Q}_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}}

(6.1)

for
s_{1}\in\{\frac{1}{2},s\}
, any
0<T\leq T_{\ast}
, and uniformly in
h_{0}\leq h\leq\infty
.
First we consider the Fourier restriction norm estimates for the difference
U_{h}:=u_{h}-u_{\infty}
. Using (
1.4
) and (
1.4
), we see that
U_{h}
satisfies

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+i\partial_{x}^{2})U_{h}=

\displaystyle 2\beta\big[u_{h}\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{h}|^{2})-u_{\infty}\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{\infty}|^{2})]+i\gamma(|u_{h}|^{2}u_{h}-|u_{\infty}|^{2}u_{\infty})-i\beta u_{h}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{h}|^{2}),

noting that there is no dependence on
u_{\infty}
on the third term on the right-hand side.
By repeating the proof of (
3.38
) and using (
6.1
), we then obtain

\displaystyle\|U_{h}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}\leq C(1+\widetilde{Q}_{0}(K)^{2}K^{2})\big(\|U_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}+\|J^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}U_{h}\|_{\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}}\big)+C\widetilde{Q}_{0}(K)^{3}K^{3}\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}},

where
C>0
is independent of
h_{0}\leq h\leq\infty
.
Proceeding analogously for
\widetilde{U}_{h}
and combining both estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\|U_{h}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}+\|\widetilde{U}_{h}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{T}}\leq C(K)\big\{\|U_{h}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|\widetilde{U}_{h}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}\big\}+C(K)\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}}.

(6.2)

Next, we estimate the differences
\widetilde{V}_{h}:=\widetilde{v}_{h}-\widetilde{v}_{\infty}
and
W_{h}:=w_{h}-w_{\infty}
.
Namely, using that
\widetilde{V}_{h}
satisfies (
5.41
) with
\widetilde{u}_{1}=\widetilde{u}_{h}
,
\widetilde{u}_{2}=\widetilde{u}_{\infty}
, and with
\mathcal{A}_{5},\mathcal{A}_{6}
replaced by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{5}=-i\beta\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[e^{i\widetilde{F_{h}}}\widetilde{u_{h}}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|\widetilde{u_{h}}|^{2})]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{A}_{6}=0,

and
W_{h}
satisfies (
5.42
) with the same modifications and with
\mathcal{B}_{3}
replaced by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{3}=-i\beta\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}[u_{h}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{h}|^{2})],

we repeat the analysis leading to (
5.46
), which together with (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widetilde{V}_{h}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|W_{h}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}&\leq C_{1}[\|\widetilde{V}_{h}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}+\|W_{h}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}]+C(K)K_{s_{1}}T^{\theta}\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}}\\
&\quad+C_{0}(K)T^{\theta}\big\{\|(U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h},\widetilde{V}_{h},W_{h})\|_{R^{s_{1}}(T)}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXXX}+(1+K_{s_{1}})(\|U_{h}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|\widetilde{U}_{h}\|_{S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)})\big\},\end{split}

(6.3)

for some
\theta>0
and constants
C,C_{1},C_{0}>0
independent of
h_{0}\leq h\leq\infty
, where we used that
\sup_{h_{0}\leq h}L_{h}\lesssim 1
. Note that the second term on the RHS of (
6.3
) comes from bounding
\mathcal{A}_{5}
and
\mathcal{B}_{3}
.
We can similarly repeat the arguments used in the proof of (
5.48
)-(
5.49
) to obtain bounds for
U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h}
in
S^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)
, which include an additional term
C(K)T\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\textup{op}}
coming from the term
\mathbf{P}_{\leq M}(u_{h}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{h}|^{2})
appearing when adapting (
3.41
) (and (
3.42
)).
Altogether, we find

\displaystyle\|(U,\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},W)\|_{R^{s_{1}}(T)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\big(1+T^{\frac{5}{8}}\Lambda^{\frac{5}{4}}C_{0}(K)K_{s_{1}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}C_{0}(K)+C_{2}(K)K_{s_{1}}\big)\|U_{h}(0)\|_{H^{s_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+T^{\frac{1}{8}}C_{0}(K))\big[\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}\widetilde{v}_{\infty}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}w_{\infty}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}(K)\big[T^{\theta}K_{s_{1}}+T^{\frac{3}{4}}M^{3}\big]\|(U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h},\widetilde{V}_{h},W_{h})\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{h}}}-e^{-i\beta\widetilde{F_{\infty}}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}\widetilde{v}_{\infty}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{P}_{\ll M}[e^{-i\beta F_{h}}-e^{-i\beta F_{\infty}}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\gtrsim M}w_{\infty}\|_{X^{s_{1},b}_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K)K_{s_{1}}T^{\theta}\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}}.

By repeating the arguments in the proof of Lemma
5.6
, involving choosing
M=M(K_{s_{1}})
sufficiently large, and then
T=T(K_{s_{1}})
sufficiently small, we get

\displaystyle\|(U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h},\widetilde{V}_{h},W_{h})\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(K,\Lambda,T)\|U_{h}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+C(K)[\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{\infty}(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}]+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}},

\displaystyle\|(U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h},\widetilde{V}_{h},W_{h})\|_{R^{s}(T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(K,\Lambda,T)\|U_{h}(0)\|_{H^{s}}+C(K)[\Lambda^{-\theta_{3}}+\|\Pi_{>\Lambda}u_{\infty}(0)\|_{H^{s}}]

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,T,M,K_{s})\|(U_{h},\widetilde{U}_{h},\widetilde{V}_{h},W_{h})\|_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}(T)}+\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{\text{op}},

for any
0<T\leq T_{0}
, and where the constants are uniform in
h_{0}\leq h\leq\infty
. Now by taking
h\to\infty
using that
\lim_{h\to\infty}\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}=0
for any
1<p<\infty
and
U_{h}(0)\to 0
in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
, and then taking
\Lambda\to\infty
, this establishes that
u_{h}\to u_{\infty}
in
C([0,T_{0}];H^{s}_{x})\cap L^{4}([0,T_{0}];W^{s,4}(\mathbb{T}))
as
h\to\infty
.
By iterating this argument, we obtain the convergence up to time
T=T_{\ast}
.

7.
Unconditional uniqueness

In this section, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
by showing the uniqueness claims in (ii)-(iii).
In particular,
we show that if
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
with
s>\frac{3}{5}
, then uniqueness holds in the class
L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
, proving (ii). By the Sobolev embedding
H^{s+\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow W^{s,4}(\mathbb{T})
, this implies (iii), namely, unconditional uniqueness of solutions in
L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}
for
s>\frac{3}{5}+\frac{1}{4}=\frac{17}{20}
.
From the uniqueness in Theorem
1.1
(i), matters are reduced to verifying that under these assumptions, the gauged variables
\widetilde{v}
and
w
, defined in (
1.8
) belong to
X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
. We broadly follow the approach in
[
50
]
; however, in order to obtain unconditional well-posedness in the energy space (
s=1
), we need to refine the argument.

In the following, let
u\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
be a solution to (
1.1
). From (
3.14
), we get that
u\in X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}
for any
\theta\in[0,1]
, and thus
w=\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}u\in X^{s-\theta,\theta}_{T}
.
If
s>1
, this suffices to show that
w\in X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
. When
\frac{1}{2}<s\leq 1
, we rely on the following crucial smoothing property for
w
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
s>\frac{1}{2}
,
u\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
be a distributional solution to (
1.1
), and
w:=\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}u
. Then,
w\in X^{\min(\frac{3s-1}{2},s)-,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
.

Proof.

First, we note that
w\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
and it solves (
3.7
).
Proceeding as in the proof of (
3.14
), we start by estimating
\|w\|_{X^{\sigma,1}_{T}}
, for some
\frac{1}{2}<\sigma\leq s
to be chosen later, which reduces to estimating the terms

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}(w\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\sigma}_{x}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|-i\beta u\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})+i\gamma|u|^{2}u\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\sigma}_{x}}.

(7.1)

The second term in (
7.1
) is estimated using the algebra property of
H^{\sigma}
,
\sigma>\frac{1}{2}
, and the boundedness of
\mathcal{G}_{h}
.
Thus, we focus on estimating the first term in (
7.1
).
By dyadic decomposition, we have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}(w\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2}))\|_{L^{2}_{T}H^{\sigma}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{N\geq 1}N^{2\sigma}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{23}\\
N_{1}\gtrsim N\vee N_{23}\end{subarray}}\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u)\big]\big\|_{L^{2}_{T,x}}\bigg)^{2}.

(7.2)

Note that the extra condition
N_{1}\gtrsim N\vee N_{23}
arises from the signs of the frequencies as we have
|\xi_{1}|=|\xi|+|\xi_{3}-\xi_{2}|
.
First, if
N_{23}\lesssim 1
, then using Hölder and Bernstein’s inequalities,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u)\big]\big\|_{L^{2}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{23}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}N_{1}^{-s}(N_{2}N_{3})^{-\frac{1}{8}}\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\prod_{j=2}^{3}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

We can then perform the dyadic sums provided that
\sigma<s
, obtaining the bound

\displaystyle\text{LHS }\eqref{uu1a}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\|w\|_{L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}^{2}.

Now we consider the case when
N_{23}\gtrsim 1
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
N_{2}\geq N_{3}
and so
N_{23}\lesssim N_{2}
.
Then, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\cdot\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u)\big]\big\|_{L^{2}_{T,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N_{23}}{N_{1}^{s}N_{2}^{s}}\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T,x}}.

In order to perform the dyadic summations, we need to control the dyadic factor which is

\displaystyle N^{\sigma}N_{23}(N_{1}N_{2})^{-s}\lesssim N^{\sigma}N_{23}^{1-s}N_{1}^{-s}\lesssim N_{1}^{1-2s+\sigma}.

This power is negative provided that
\sigma<2s-1
, and we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\text{LHS }\eqref{uu1a}\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}.

This show that
w\in X^{\min(2s-1,s)-,1}_{T}
. Interpolating this with
w\in X^{s,0}_{T}
we get
w\in X^{\min(\frac{3s-1}{2},s)-,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
, as intended.
∎

At this point, Lemma
7.1
already implies that
w\in X_{T}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}
for
s>\frac{2}{3}
.
The following nonlinear estimate allows us to further improve this restriction (to
s>\frac{3}{5}
).

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}<s\leq 1
,
1-s<\sigma\leq s
, and
0<\delta\ll 1
. Then,
it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}&\mathbf{P}_{-\textup{hi}}[w\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})]\|_{X^{\sigma,-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}}\\
&\lesssim\|w\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}}\big(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H_{x}^{s}}+\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}+\|u\|_{X^{s-1,1}_{T}})^{2}.\end{split}

(7.3)

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of (
4.1
)-(
4.2
).
By duality and dyadic decomposition, we bound

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{-,\text{hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}w)\,\mathbf{P}_{+}\mathbf{P}_{N_{23}}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u)]dxdt

(7.4)

under the conditions (
4.5
) and (
4.6
), where
g\in X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}
, for some small
\delta>0
. When
N_{2}\sim N_{3}
, we argue as in Case 1 of (
4.1
), noting that since
s>\frac{1}{2}
, we can use
L^{4}_{T,x}
for
u
instead of the slightly stronger
\widetilde{L^{4}_{T,x}}
. When
N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\land N_{3}\gtrsim N
, we use Hölder’s inequality and (
3.2
), to bound

(
7.4
)

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma}N_{23}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma}N_{23}N_{1}^{-\sigma}N_{2}^{-s}N_{3}^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}.

We then control the dyadic factor as follows:

\displaystyle N^{\sigma}N_{23}N_{1}^{-\sigma}N_{2}^{-s}N_{3}^{-s}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{2}\land N_{3})^{\sigma-s}(N_{2}\lor N_{3})^{1-s}N_{1}^{-\sigma}\lesssim N_{\max}^{1-s-\sigma}

since
\sigma\leq s
,
which is a negative power provided that
\sigma>1-s
.

Now we consider the case when
N_{2}\vee N_{3}\gg N_{2}\wedge N_{3}
and
N_{2}\land N_{3}\ll N
. Here we have both (
4.8
) and (
4.9
). We decompose according to the modulation variables as in (
4.10
)-(
4.13
) (note that by the same argument, the corresponding term (
4.14
) will be identically zero due to impossible interactions).
As in the bound for (
4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{\sigma}\overline{\mathbf{Q}_{\gtrsim K}\mathbf{P}_{N}g}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{+,hi}}[\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w_{T}\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u}\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u)]dxdt\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma}K^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}N_{23}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}\vee N_{3}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}\wedge N_{3}}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma+\delta}K^{-\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}N_{23}N_{1}^{-\sigma}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{3}{8}}}\|J_{x}^{s}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{HI}}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}.

The resulting dyadic multiplier is then

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\sigma+\delta}N_{23}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}N_{1}^{\sigma}(N_{2}\vee N_{3})^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma-\frac{1}{2}+3\delta}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}+2\delta-s}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{4}},

given that
\delta>0
is sufficiently small. The contribution for the corresponding term of the form (
4.11
) when
w
has high modulation is handled analogously, by placing
(g,w)\in L^{4}_{t,x}\times L^{2}_{t,x}
, which leads us to controlling the following dyadic multiplier

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\sigma}N_{23}(N_{2}\land N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s+}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{\sigma}(N_{2}\lor N_{3})^{s}}\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma-\frac{1}{2}}N_{23}^{\frac{1}{2}-s}(N_{2}\land N_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}-s+}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

When
\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u
has high modulation as in (
4.12
), we have

\displaystyle N^{\sigma+\delta}N_{23}N_{1}^{-\sigma}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}}}K^{-1}N_{2}^{1-s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\|_{X^{s-1,1}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the dyadic factor, we have

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\sigma+\delta}N_{23}N_{2}^{1-s}}{KN_{1}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma+\delta}N_{23}(N_{2}\lor N_{3})^{1-s}}{N_{23}NN_{1}^{\sigma}}\lesssim N^{\sigma+\delta-1}N_{1}^{1-s-\sigma}\lesssim N_{\max}^{1-s-\sigma+\delta}

where we used
1-s<\sigma\leq 1
.
Finally, we have the term when
\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u
has a high
modulation, as in (
4.13
), where we consider the further case separation
N_{2}\ll N_{3}
or
N_{2}\gg N_{3}
. If
N_{2}\ll N_{3}
, we use Hölder’s inequality, (
3.2
), (
3.3
) since
K\sim NN_{3}\gg N_{2}^{2}
, to control the quantity by

\displaystyle N^{\sigma}N_{23}N^{\frac{1}{2}+}K^{2\delta+}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}g\|_{X^{0,\frac{1}{2}-2\delta}}N_{1}^{-\sigma}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}w\|_{X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+}}N_{2}^{-s}\|J^{s}\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u\|_{L^{4}_{T,x}}N_{3}^{1-s}K^{-1}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{3}}u\|_{X^{s-1,1}},

where it remains to estimate the multiplier

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\sigma+\frac{1}{2}+}N_{23}N_{3}^{1-s}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}K^{1-2\delta-}N_{2}^{s}}\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma+\frac{1}{2}+}N_{3}^{1-s+2\delta+}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}(N\lor N_{3})^{1-2\delta-}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-s+4\delta+}

since
N\lor N_{3}\sim N_{1}\sim N_{\max}
, where the power is negative for
s>\frac{1}{2}
and
\delta>0
sufficiently small. If
N_{2}\gg N_{3}
, we instead use (
3.2
) to place the
\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u
-term in
X^{s-\frac{3}{8},\frac{3}{8}}
, giving the following multiplier

\displaystyle\frac{N^{\sigma+\frac{1}{2}+}N_{23}N_{3}^{1-s}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}K^{1-2\delta-}N_{2}^{s-\frac{3}{8}}}\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma+\frac{1}{2}+}N_{3}^{1-s}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}N_{2}^{s-\frac{3}{8}-2\delta-}N^{1-2\delta-}}\lesssim\frac{N^{\sigma+\frac{1}{2}+2\delta-s+}}{N_{1}^{\sigma}}\lesssim N_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}+2\delta-s+},

which again has a negative power.
This completes the proof. ∎

Lastly, we combine the earlier results to prove unconditional uniqueness.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
(ii).

Let
s>\frac{3}{5}
and
u\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
be a solution to (
1.1
).
We first show that
w=\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}u
is in
X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
.
If
s>1
, we argue as discussed at the start of Section
7
. In the following, we take
\frac{3}{5}<s\leq 1
.
From the conditions on Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.2
, we want to take
1-s<\sigma<\min(\frac{3s-1}{2},s)=\frac{3s-1}{2}
, which requires that
s>\frac{3}{5}
. Then, using Lemma
7.2
to estimate the first term on the RHS of (
3.7
) and (
3.2
) for the remaining ones,
we can run a contraction mapping argument to obtain a unique solution to (
3.7
) in
X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T^{\prime}}
where
\sigma=\frac{3s-1}{2}-\delta
, for fixed
u
, with initial data
\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}u_{0}
, and for some
0<T^{\prime}\leq T
.
Similarly, using Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
, we can solve equation (
3.7
) via a contraction mapping argument in
X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}
to obtain a unique solution
w^{\prime}\in X^{s,\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
. Since
\sigma\leq s
, we have that
w^{\prime}\in X^{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}
, and by the earlier uniqueness, we must have

w^{\prime}=w=\mathbf{P}_{-,\textup{hi}}u\in X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
.

We now move onto showing that
\widetilde{v}:=\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}[e^{i\beta F[u]}u](t,x-2\beta\mathbf{P}_{0}(|u_{0}|^{2})t)\in X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
. In order to show that
\widetilde{v}
solves (
3.10
), we need to first check that
\mathbf{P}_{0}(|u(t)|^{2})
is conserved.
To prove this, we fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and consider the truncated variable
u_{N}(t):=\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}u(t)
. Then,
u_{N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})
and solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{N}+i\partial_{x}^{2}u_{N}

\displaystyle=2\beta u_{N}\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{N}|^{2})-i\beta u_{N}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{N}|^{2})+i\gamma|u_{N}|^{2}u_{N}+R_{N},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\text{where }\quad R_{N}

\displaystyle=2\beta\big\{\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}[u\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})]-u_{N}\mathbf{P}_{+}\partial_{x}(|u_{N}|^{2})\big\}

\displaystyle\quad-i\beta\big\{\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}[u\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})]-u_{N}\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u_{N}|^{2})\big\}+i\gamma\big\{\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}[|u|^{2}u]-|u_{N}|^{2}u_{N}\big\}.

From the definition of the mass in (
1.13
) and (
7.5
), it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}M(u_{N}(t))=2\text{Re}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\overline{u_{N}(t)}R_{N}dx.

Let
\frac{1}{2}<s_{0}<s
, which implies that
1-s_{0}\leq s
.
Using Cauchy-Schwarz and (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|2\text{Re}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\overline{u_{N}(t)}R_{N}dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{N}\|_{H^{1-s_{0}}}\|R_{N}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}}\leq C_{h}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}})\big[\|u-u_{N}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}+N^{-(s-s_{0})}]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}_{h}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}})N^{-(s-s_{0})}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle M(u_{N}(t))=M(\mathbf{P}_{\leq N}u_{0})+O(N^{-(s-s_{0})})

(7.6)

and since
u_{N}(t)\to u(t)
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
for each fixed
t\in[0,T]
, as
N\to\infty
, we conclude that
M(u(t))=M(u_{0})
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

It follows from Lemma
2.1
that
\widetilde{v}\in L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{T}W^{s,4}_{x}
. Moreover as
s>\frac{1}{2}
, one can check that
\widetilde{v}
solves (
3.10
) in
L^{\infty}_{T}H^{s-2}_{x}
. Then, by repeating the argument in Lemma
7.1
, we get
\widetilde{v}\in X^{\max(\frac{3s-1}{2}+\delta,s),\frac{1}{2}+\delta}_{T}
. In particular, we are done if
s>1
. If
s\leq 1
, then we follow the same argument as we did for
w
and note that (
7.3
) also holds for the nonlinear term
\mathbf{P}_{+,\textup{hi}}[\widetilde{v}\,\mathbf{P}_{-}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})].

We thus get
\widetilde{v}\in X^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}+}_{T}
provided that
s>\frac{3}{5}
. This completes the proof of unconditional uniqueness for (
1.1
).
∎

8.
Global well-posedness

We start by showing global well-posedness in the energy space.

Proof of Corollary
1.3
.

The global-in-time result of Corollary
1.3
follows from iterating the local argument of Theorem
1.1
once we derive an a priori bound on the
H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
norm of solutions to (
1.1
).
Let
u
be a smooth solution to (
1.1
) with
u(0)=u_{0}
. From the energy in (
1.11
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\partial_{x}u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=E(u_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big\{\beta|u|^{2}\Im(u\partial_{x}\overline{u})-\tfrac{\beta}{2}|u|^{2}\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})-\tfrac{\gamma}{2}|u|^{4}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{3}|u|^{6}\big\}dx

(8.1)

For the first term in the integral, from Hölder’s and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\beta|u|^{2}\Im(u\partial_{x}\overline{u})dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq|\beta|\|u\|^{3}_{L^{6}}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq\tfrac{|\beta|^{2}}{2\varepsilon_{0}}\|u\|^{6}_{L^{6}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(8.2)

In the defocusing case,
\beta>0
, from (
8.1
) with (
8.2
), and the positive-definiteness of the operator
\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq E(u_{0})+\tfrac{\gamma^{2}}{8(2\pi)\varepsilon_{1}}M(u_{0})+\big(\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2\varepsilon_{0}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}-\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{3}\big)\|u\|^{6}_{L^{6}}+\tfrac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

where we used that
\frac{|\gamma|}{2}|u|^{4}\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}}{8\varepsilon_{1}}|u|^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}|u|^{6}
for
\varepsilon_{1}>0
, and the conservation of mass in (
1.13
). Thus, by choosing
\frac{3}{2}<\varepsilon_{0}<2
and
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(\varepsilon_{0},\beta)>0
sufficiently small, we obtain the intended a priori bound.

Next, we consider the focusing case,
\beta<0
.
In this case, we need to estimate the second term in the integral in (
8.1
). In particular,
from Hölder’s inequality,
L^{p}
-boundedness of
\mathcal{T}_{h}
, Gagliardo-Nirenberg-Sobolev inequality
[
43
, Lemma 4.1]
, and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}|u|^{2}\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\leq\|u\|^{2}_{L^{6}}\|\mathcal{T}_{h}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{h}\|u\|^{3}_{L^{6}}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{h}\big((C_{\textup{GNS}}+\varepsilon)\|u\|^{4}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{\varepsilon}\|u\|^{6}_{L^{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{h}(C_{\textup{GNS}}+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{h}C_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|^{3}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{h}\big\{(C_{\textup{GNS}}+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}M(u_{0})+\varepsilon^{\prime}\big\}\|\partial_{x}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{h}^{2}C_{\varepsilon}C_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}M(u_{0})^{3}\,

where
C_{\textup{GNS}}>0
denotes the optimal constant in the Gagliardo-Nirenberg-Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{R}
,
C_{h}>0
depends on
\|\mathcal{T}_{h}\|_{L^{p}\to L^{p}}
,
0<\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq 1
, and
C_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}>0
depend only on
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}
, respectively.
Thus, proceeding as in the defocusing case, combining (
8.1
), (
8.2
), and the estimate above, for
\varepsilon_{0}
as before,
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon_{1}>0
and
M(u_{0})>0
sufficiently small, we obtain an a priori bound for
\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\partial_{x}u|^{2}dx
, completing the proof.
∎

Next, we turn to the
\gamma=0
case of (
1.1
):

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+i\partial_{x}^{2}u=2\beta u\Pi_{+,h}\partial_{x}\big(|u|^{2}\big),

(8.3)

where

\Pi_{+,h}=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+i\mathcal{T}_{h}).

Our goal is to establish a priori estimates in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
(see Theorem
8.4
below), and so finish the proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Proposition 8.1
.

For any
0<h\leq\infty
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
,
u(t)
solves (
8.3
) on the circle if and only if the operators

\mathcal{L}_{u;h}=-i\partial_{x}+\beta u(\Pi_{+,h}+c\mathbf{P}_{0})\overline{u}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{P}_{u;h}=-i\partial_{x}^{2}+2\beta u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,h}\overline{u}

on
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
satisfy

\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u;h}f=[\mathcal{P}_{u;h},\mathcal{L}_{u;h}]f+\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}u\mathbf{P}_{0}\big[(1+\mathcal{T}_{h}^{2})\partial_{x}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\cdot\overline{u}f\big]+\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{2}u(1+\mathcal{T}_{h}^{2})\partial_{x}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{0}(\overline{u}f)

(8.4)

for any
f\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
.

The proof of Proposition
8.1
follows an argument parallel to the line case in
[
10
, Proposition 6.1]
, thus we omit details. We note that, on
\mathbb{T}
, the operators
\mathcal{L}_{u;h},\mathcal{P}_{u;h}
only constitute an
almost
Lax pair, due to the second and third terms on the right-hand side of (
8.4
). These arise from the application of the following Cotlar-type identity on the circle

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{h}[(\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}g)\mathcal{T}_{h}k+(\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}k)\mathcal{T}_{h}g]=\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}\big[\mathcal{T}_{h}g\cdot\mathcal{T}_{h}k-(\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}g)(\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}k)\big],

(8.5)

as mean zero corrections needed for (
8.5
) to apply.
However, the Lax relation is recovered in the limit
h=\infty
, as we see that (
8.4
) becomes

\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}=[\mathcal{P}_{u;\infty},\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}],

(8.6)

for a solution
u
to (
1.6
),
since
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}^{2}=\mathcal{H}^{2}=-\mathbf{P}_{\neq 0}
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}\partial_{x}(|u|^{2})=0
.
In this case, we take the Lax pair as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}

\displaystyle=-i\partial_{x}+\beta u\Pi_{+}\overline{u},\qquad\mathcal{P}_{u;\infty}=-i\partial_{x}^{2}+2\beta u\partial_{x}\Pi_{+}\overline{u},

(8.7)

where the projector
\Pi_{+}
is given by

\displaystyle\Pi_{+}:=\mathbf{P}_{+}+\mathbf{P}_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\textup{Id}+i\mathcal{H}+\mathbf{P}_{0}).

(8.8)

Note that (
8.7
) comes from Proposition
8.1
, by setting
h=\infty
,
c=\frac{1}{2}
, and writing
\partial_{x}\Pi_{+,\infty}=\partial_{x}\Pi_{+}
. We make these choices to have agreement with the operators that have appeared previously in the literature for CCM (
1.6
), while having no effect in either (
8.4
) or (
8.6
).

The following result summarizes what we will need about the Lax operator. Note that these results are new, as the potential
u
is not required to lie in a Hardy space. We omit the details however, since the proof is identical to that in the line case from our previous work. See
[
10
, Proposition 6.2]
.

Proposition 8.2

(Lax operator)
.

Fix
0<h\leq\infty
. Given
u\in H^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{T})
, the operator

\mathcal{L}_{u;h}f=-i\partial_{x}f+\beta u(\Pi_{+,h}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}_{0})(\overline{u}f)

with domain
H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
is self-adjoint. Moreover, there is a constant
C\geq 1
so that whenever

\kappa\geq C\max\big(\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{4}\big),

(8.9)

we have

\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq\mathcal{L}_{u;h}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\leq\tfrac{3}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

(8.10)

as quadratic forms, where
\mathcal{L}_{0}:=\mathcal{L}_{0;h}=-i\partial_{x}
.

Since for
0<h<\infty
, the pair of operators
\mathcal{L}_{u;h},\mathcal{P}_{u;h}
on
\mathbb{T}
do not form an exact Lax pair, in particular,
the relation (
8.4
) presents a rank-two perturbation of the usual Lax relation.
Instead, we treat (
8.3
) as a perturbation of the limiting case
h=\infty
, where the relation (
8.6
) holds exactly.
Specifically, (
8.3
) is equivalent to

\partial_{t}u+i\partial_{x}^{2}u=2\beta u\Pi_{+}\partial_{x}\big(|u|^{2}\big)-i\beta u\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big),

(8.11)

where
\mathcal{G}_{h}
is as in (
1.3
).
Note that this operator satisfies
\overline{\mathcal{G}_{h}(f)}=\mathcal{G}_{h}(\overline{f})
, and so the function
\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)
appearing in (
8.11
) is real-valued.
Comparing (
8.11
) with (
1.6
), we see that the equations agree up to the second term on the right-hand side of (
8.11
).
Moreover, given a smooth solution
u
of (
8.11
),
we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{u;\infty}u-i\beta u\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big),

(8.12)

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u,\infty}f

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{P}_{u;\infty},\mathcal{L}_{u,\infty}]f+iu\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}f.

(8.13)

Algebraically, this represents a further departure from the Lax pair relation (
8.6
). Analytically though, (
8.13
) is preferable to (
8.4
) for proving a priori estimates, since the operator
\mathcal{G}_{h}
and the commutator
[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})]
are smoothing, as shown in the following lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

For any
0<h\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big\|_{H^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

(8.14)

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]f\big\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.

(8.15)

Moreover, the implicit constants can be chosen uniformly for
1\leq h\leq\infty
.

Proof.

The estimate (
8.14
) follows from the smoothing property of the
\mathcal{G}_{h}
operator which maps
H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{T})
to
H^{s_{2}}(\mathbb{T})
for any
s_{1}\leq s_{2}
(see
[
11
, Lemma 2.1]
, for example) together with the embedding
L^{1}(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow H^{-1}(\mathbb{T})
:

\big\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big\|_{H^{3}}\lesssim\big\||u|^{2}\big\|_{H^{-1}}\lesssim\big\||u|^{2}\big\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Next, we turn our attention to (
8.15
). As a first step, we claim that for any
\sigma\geq 0
we have

\big\|[\mathcal{H},g]f\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|g\|_{H^{\sigma+1}}\|f\|_{H^{-\sigma}}.

(8.16)

In Fourier variables, this is equivalent to the estimate

\bigg\|\sum_{\xi=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}}\frac{\langle\xi_{2}\rangle^{\sigma}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{\sigma+1}}(\textup{sgn}(\xi)-\textup{sgn}(\xi_{2}))u(\xi_{1})v(\xi_{2})\bigg\|_{\ell^{2}_{\xi}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\ell^{2}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}}.

(8.17)

If
\xi_{2}\neq 0
, the factor
\textup{sgn}(\xi)-\textup{sgn}(\xi_{2})
is only nonzero when
|\xi_{1}|\geq|\xi_{2}|
, and thus by Young’s inequality, we have

LHS (
8.17
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(\langle\xi\rangle^{-1}u)\ast v\big\|_{\ell^{2}}\leq\|\langle\xi\rangle^{-1}u\|_{\ell^{1}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\ell^{2}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}},

while if
\xi_{2}=0
, it reduces to

\text{LHS \eqref{calderon}}=\big\|\langle\xi\rangle^{-\sigma-1}u(\xi)v(0)\big\|_{\ell^{2}_{\xi}}\leq\|u\|_{\ell^{2}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}}.

Thus, (
8.17
) follows, and so too does (
8.16
).

We can now consider
[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})]
, with
\Pi_{+}
as in (
8.8
). Since the contributions from
\frac{1}{2}[1+\mathbf{P}_{0},\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})]
are harmless, we omit details, and focus on those from
\frac{1}{2}[\mathcal{H},\mathcal{G}_{h}(|u|^{2})]
.
Combining (
8.14
) and the
\sigma=1
case of (
8.16
), we deduce

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]f\big\|_{L^{2}}+\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\partial_{x}\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]f\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.

On the other hand, the
\sigma=2
case of (
8.16
) implies

\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\partial_{x}f\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}f\|_{H^{-2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.

Collecting the previous three inequalities, we obtain (
8.15
).
∎

We are now equipped to prove our a priori estimates.

Theorem 8.4

(A priori estimates)
.

Let
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
and
0<h\leq\infty
. Then, there exists
r>0
such that for any
A,T>0
, there exists
B>0
so that

\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}\leq r\quad\text{and}\quad\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}\leq A\quad\implies\quad\sup_{|t|\leq T}\,\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}\leq B,

(8.18)

for all (global)
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
solutions to (
8.3
).
Moreover, the constants
r
and
B
can be chosen uniformly for
1\leq h\leq\infty
.

Proof.

Fix
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
, recall that
\mathcal{L}_{0}=-i\partial_{x}
, and set
\kappa
as in (
8.9
). Using the short-hand notation
\mathcal{L}_{u}=\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}
, we consider

F(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}):=\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s}u\big\rangle.

By Loewner’s Theorem, the function
x\mapsto x^{s}
on
(0,\infty)
is operator monotone (see
[
57
]
for details).
In particular, the relation (
8.10
) implies

F\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\big)\leq F(\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq F\big(\tfrac{3}{2}(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\big),

and so

\tfrac{1}{C}F(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq F(\mathcal{L}_{u;\infty}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq CF(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

(8.19)

for some constant
C>0
.

Next, we will compute the time evolution of
F(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})
.
Let
u
be a global
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})
-solution to (
8.3
). For any
E\geq 0
, a simple residue computation demonstrates

\frac{\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{E\lambda^{s-1}}{E+\lambda}\,d\lambda=E^{s}.

Consequently, if
A
is a positive self-adjoint operator and
f
is in the domain of
A
, then, by the functional calculus, we have

A^{s}f=\frac{\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}(A+\lambda)^{-1}Af\,d\lambda.

We apply this to
A=\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}
, which is positive by (
8.10
). Combining this with the resolvent identity and (
8.13
), we find

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}f={}

\displaystyle\frac{\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\big(\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}\big)f\,d\lambda

\displaystyle-\frac{\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\big(\tfrac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}\big)(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})f\,d\lambda

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{P}_{u;\infty},\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}\big]f

\displaystyle+i(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s-1}\mathcal{L}_{u}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\big(\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}f\big\}

\displaystyle+i(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s-1}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}f\big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{i\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{u}\Big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}f\big]\Big\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle-\frac{i\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\Big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}f\big]\Big\}\,d\lambda.

Combing this with (
8.12
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\tfrac{d}{dt}F\big(

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)=\tfrac{d}{dt}\big\langle u,\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}u\big\rangle

\displaystyle={-2}\beta\Im\big\langle u\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big),\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}u\big\rangle

(8.20)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+i\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s-1}\mathcal{L}_{u}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]|u|^{2}\big\}\big\rangle

(8.21)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+i\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s-1}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\big\}\big\rangle

(8.22)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{u}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\}\big\rangle\,d\lambda

(8.23)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\sin(\pi s)}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\big\langle u,(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda)^{-1}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\}\big\rangle\,d\lambda.

(8.24)

We will estimate the contributions from (
8.20
)–(
8.24
) individually.
First, for
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the norms

\|f\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}^{2}:=\|(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{\xi}|\widehat{f}(\xi)|^{2}(\xi^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\sigma}.

The
H^{\sigma}_{\kappa}
-norms are particularly useful because (
8.10
) and Loewner’s theorem imply that

\big\|(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}f\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\big\langle f,(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2})^{\sigma}f\big\rangle\sim\|f\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}^{2}

uniformly for
-1\leq\sigma\leq 1
and
\kappa
satisfying (
8.9
). These norms are equivalent to the usual
H^{\sigma}
-norm for fixed
\kappa\geq 1

and they enjoy similar estimates, such as

\|fg\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{\sigma}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\qquad\text{for}\qquad\sigma\geq\max(s,\tfrac{1}{2}+),

(8.25)

where the constants are uniform in
\kappa\geq 1
.

We begin with (
8.20
). Using (
8.25
) and (
8.14
), we bound

\displaystyle|\eqref{ap1}|\lesssim\big\|u\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\big\|\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big\|_{H^{s+1}}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Next, we turn to (
8.21
). Using (
8.15
), we find

\displaystyle\big\|u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}f\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}f\big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{3}\|\overline{u}f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

(8.26)

Employing this with
f=u
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\eqref{ap5}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\big\|(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}-1}\mathcal{L}_{u}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]|u|^{2}\big\}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\big\|(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]|u|^{2}\big\}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\big\|u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]|u|^{2}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{5}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Let us skip now to (
8.23
), as it also uses (
8.26
). Applying (
8.26
) to
f=\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u
and following an argument parallel to that for (
8.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\eqref{ap3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}\big\|(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}_{u}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\}\big\|_{L^{2}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}\big\|u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\|_{L^{2}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}}\big\|\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\|_{L^{2}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{5}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\lambda^{s-1}\,d\lambda+\int_{1}^{\infty}\lambda^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}\,d\lambda\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

It remains to estimate (
8.22
) and (
8.24
). Using (
2.6
) and Sobolev embedding, we bound

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\overline{u}\partial_{x}f\|_{H^{-1}}+\big\||u|^{2}\Pi_{+}\big(\overline{u}f\big)\big\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|f\|_{H^{s}}+\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{3}\|f\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|f\|_{H^{s}}+\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{3}\|f\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|f\|_{H^{s}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{3-\frac{3}{4s}}\|u\|_{H^{s}}^{\frac{3}{4s}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{1}{4s}}\|f\|_{H^{s}}^{\frac{1}{4s}}.

(8.27)

To estimate (
8.22
), we apply (
8.27
) with
f=u
, to obtain

\|\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\|_{H^{-1}}\lesssim\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}

uniformly in
\kappa\geq 1
. Combining this with (
8.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\eqref{ap4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big\|(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2})^{s-1}\big\{u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\big\}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big\|u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}u\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

For (
8.24
), we will take
f=\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u
in (
8.27
). Note that

\big\|\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\big\|\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}u\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\big\|\big(\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}u\big\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}

for any
-1\leq\sigma\leq 1
, and so (
8.27
) yields

\|\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\|_{H^{-1}}\lesssim\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}

uniformly for
\kappa\geq 1
. Following an argument parallel to that in the previous paragraph for (
8.23
), we see that

\displaystyle|\eqref{ap2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1}\big\|u\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\|_{L^{2}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1}\big\|\big[\Pi_{+},\mathcal{G}_{h}\big(|u|^{2}\big)\big]\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-1}\langle\lambda\rangle^{-1}\|\overline{u}\mathcal{L}_{u}\tfrac{\mathcal{L}_{u}^{2}+\kappa^{2}}{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{u}+\kappa^{2}+\lambda}u\|_{H^{-1}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\lambda^{s-1}\,d\lambda+\int_{1}^{\infty}\lambda^{s-2}\,d\lambda\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(1+\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|u\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2},

since
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
.

Collecting the previous estimates, we obtain

\tfrac{d}{dt}F(\mathcal{L}_{u(t)}^{2}+\kappa^{2})\leq K\big(\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}}\big)F(\mathcal{L}_{u(t)}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

for some polynomial
K
with no constant term. Note that
K(\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}})=K(\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}})=K
is constant in time, by conservation of the
L^{2}
-norm. Combining the Gronwall inequality with (
8.19
), this yields

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\langle u(t),\big(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}u(t)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq CF(\mathcal{L}_{u(t)}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{K|t|}F(\mathcal{L}_{u(0)}^{2}+\kappa^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}e^{K|t|}\big\langle u(0),\big(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\big)^{s}u(0)\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}e^{K|t|}\big(\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\kappa^{2s}\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

(8.28)

Now we will prove (
8.18
)
in the case
T=1
using a bootstrap argument. Later in the argument we will iterate this to cover the full interval
[0,T]
, for
T\geq 1
.
Given
u(0)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
with
u(0)\neq 0
, set

\kappa=C\max\big(\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}},4C^{2}e^{K}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2s}}.

Here,
C\geq 1
is larger than the constants from (
8.9
) and (
8.28
).
Also, let
0<r\leq 1
, so that

\|u(0)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{4}\leq\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{4-\frac{1}{s}}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{\frac{1}{s}}\leq\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{\frac{1}{s}}.

(8.29)

For this
\kappa
and any time interval
[0,t_{0}]\subset[0,1]
on which

\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}\leq 2Ce^{\frac{K}{2}}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}},

(8.30)

the condition (
8.29
) implies that (
8.9
) holds, and thus so too does the monotonicity relation (
8.10
). Then, (
8.28
) yields

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}e^{K}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C^{2+2s}e^{K}\max\big(\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}},4C^{2}e^{K}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\big)\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}e^{K}\big\{1+C^{2s}\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}\max\big(1,4C^{2}e^{K}\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}\big)\big\}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Taking
r
possibly smaller so that
r^{2}\ll C^{-2-2s}e^{-K}
, we deduce

\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\tfrac{3}{2}Ce^{\frac{K}{2}}\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}.

(8.31)

Comparing this with (
8.30
), we conclude that (
8.31
) holds for all
t\in[0,1]
.

Finally, we iterate this argument to cover an arbitrary time interval
[0,T]
. We choose
r^{2}\ll C^{-2-2s}e^{-K}
for the time interval
[0,1]
as before, depending only on
\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}
. In turn, this constant
r
guarantees that (
8.31
) holds, and so the
H^{s}
-norm can grow to at most

\|u(1)\|_{H^{s}}\leq R\|u(0)\|_{H^{s}}\quad\text{with}\quad R=\tfrac{3}{2}Ce^{\frac{K}{2}}.

Turning to the time interval
[1,2]
, we now take

\kappa=C\max\big(\|u(1)\|_{L^{2}},4C^{2}e^{K}\|u(1)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2s}},

and deduce

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq C^{2}e^{K}\big\{1+C^{2s}\|u(1)\|_{L^{2}}\max\big(1,4C^{2}e^{K}\|u(1)\|_{L^{2}}\big)\big\}\|u(1)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

By conservation of the
L^{2}
-norm, the value of
\|u(1)\|_{L^{2}}
is still bounded by
r
, and so precisely the same choice of
r^{2}\ll C^{-2-2s}e^{-K}
closes the bootstrap argument on
[1,2]
. In this way, the argument may be iterated indefinitely. After
N=\lceil T\rceil\geq 1
iterations, we conclude that the
H^{s}
-norm can grow to at most
B=R^{N}A
, completing the proof.
∎
